still converges to a central (non-central, resp.)
\chi^{2}_{r}
under the null (resp. local alternative) hypothesis. A precise statement of this result is left to the interested reader. We note, however, that a fixed value of
r
does not lead to a consistent test procedure as it can fail to reject the null hypothesis when
X_{i}\not=X_{j}
if their difference does not manifest itself in the coordinates of the leading
r
eigenvectors of
P
. The case when
\kappa
is an infinite rank kernel with
r\rightarrow\infty
is more complicated as computing
T_{4}
requires inverting an
r\times r
estimated covariance matrix
\widehat{\Sigma}(\widehat{X}_{i})+\widehat{\Sigma}(\widehat{X}_{j})
, which then introduce additional constraints on the rate at which
r
can grow with
n\rho_{n}
. We leave the precise analysis of this dependency, and its effects on the limiting distribution for
T_{4}
, to the interested reader.
∎

(a)
n=4000

(b)
n=8000

Empirical histograms, based on
500
Monte Carlo replicates, for
T(\widehat{X}_{i},\widehat{X}_{j})
under
\mathbb{H}_{0}\colon X_{i}=X_{j}
when the link function is
\kappa(x,y)=\exp(-\|x-y\|)
, the latent positions
\{X_{i}\}
are sampled i.i.d. from the uniform distribution on the unit sphere in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, and the sparsity parameter is
\rho_{n}=0.4
. The red curve in each plot is the probability density function for a weighted sum of
\widehat{r}
independent
\chi^{2}_{1}
random variables as given in
Eq.

4.16
, where
\widehat{r}
is chosen according to
Eq.

4.12
resulting in
\widehat{r}\equiv 4
when
n=4000
and
\widehat{r}\equiv 9
when
n=8000
.

Monte Carlo replicates, for
T(\widehat{X}_{i},\widehat{X}_{j})
under
\mathbb{H}_{0}\colon X_{i}=X_{j}
when the link function is
\kappa(x,y)=\exp(-\tfrac{5}{2}\|x-y\|^{2})
, the latent positions
\{X_{i}\}
are sampled i.i.d. from the bivariate normal distribution with mean zero and identity covariance matrix, and the sparsity parameter is
\rho_{n}=0.4
. The red curve in each plot is the probability density function for a weighted sum of
\widehat{r}
independent
\chi^{2}_{1}
random variables as given in
Eq.

4.16
, where
\widehat{r}
is chosen via the criteria
Eq.

4.12
resulting in
\widehat{r}\equiv 3
when
n=2000
and
\widehat{r}\equiv 6
when
n=4000
.

5
Numerical examples

We conduct several simulations to evaluate the finite-sample performance of the test statistic studied in
Theorem

4
.

For our first simulation, we sample a collection of
n-1
points
\{X_{1},\dots,X_{n-1}\}
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
according to a uniform distribution on the unit sphere, with
n\in\{4000,8000\}
. We set
X_{n}=X_{1}
and let
p_{ij}=\rho\exp(-\|X_{i}-X_{j}\|)
, where
\rho=0.4
. Given
P=(p_{ij})
, we sample
500
independent realizations of
A
from
P
. For each realization
A
we obtain
\widehat{U}
, the matrix formed by the
\widehat{r}
leading eigenvectors of
A
, where
\widehat{r}
is chosen according to
Eq.

4.12
, and then finally compute
T(\widehat{X}_{1},\widehat{X}_{n})
. For the link function specified here, the eigenvalues
\mu_{r}
converge to zero at rate
r^{-3/2}

[
50
]
. Histograms of
T(\widehat{X}_{1},\widehat{X}_{n})
based on these
500
realizations of
A
are shown in
Fig.

2
. These histograms indicate that the empirical distributions of
T(\widehat{X}_{1},\widehat{X}_{n})
are well-approximated in finite samples by weighted sums of independent
\chi^{2}_{1}
as specified in
Theorem

4
. We emphasize that, to make this simulation example fully data dependent, the weights are obtained from the eigenvalues of
\widehat{U}^{\top}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}\widehat{U}
. See
Eq.

4.16
.

For our second simulation, we sample a collection of
n-1
points
\{X_{1},\dots,X_{n-1}\}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
according to a bivariate normal distribution with mean zero and identity covariance matrix. We set
X_{n}=X_{1}
and let
P
be the matrix whose entries are
p_{ij}=\rho\exp(-\|X_{i}-X_{j}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2})
, where
\rho=0.4
and
\sigma^{2}=0.4
. Given
P
, we sample
500
independent realizations of
A
from
P
. For each realization
A
we obtain
\widehat{U}
, the matrix formed by the
r
leading eigenvectors of
A
, where
\widehat{r}
is chosen according to
Eq.

4.12
, and then finally compute
T(\widehat{X}_{1},\widehat{X}_{n})
. Histograms of
T(\widehat{X}_{1},\widehat{X}_{n})
based on these
500
realizations of
A
are shown in
Fig.

3
. These histograms again indicate that the empirical distributions of
T(\widehat{X}_{1},\widehat{X}_{n})
are well-approximated in finite samples by weighted sums of independent
\chi^{2}_{1}
as specified in
Theorem

4
. As above, the weights are obtained from the eigenvalues of
\widehat{U}^{\top}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}\widehat{U}
as described in
Eq.

4.16
.

Table 2:
Empirical estimate of power for testing equality of latent positions with Gaussian kernel.

Finally, we evaluate the empirical size and power of our proposed test statistic. The settings here are the same as described above except that we now sample
X_{n}
so that
\|X_{n}-X_{1}\|=\epsilon
for
\epsilon\in\{0,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,1\}
. For each realization
A
, we compute
T_{1n}=T(\widehat{X}_{1},\widehat{X}_{n})
and reject the null hypothesis whenever
T_{1n}
exceeds the
0.95
quantile of the distribution described in
Eq.

4.16
. The results are presented in
Tables

2
and
1
for several combinations of
n
and
\rho_{n}
. Each entry in these tables is a sample average based on
500
Monte Carlo replicates. The entries in column
\epsilon=0
are the empirical size of the test statistic under the null hypothesis, while those in the columns with
\epsilon>0
are the empirical power under the alternative hypothesis. The last column in each table records the embedding dimensions
\widehat{r}
(chosen according to the criteria in
Eq.

4.12
) and their frequency. For example, in
Table

2
with
n=500
and
\rho_{n}=0.8
, we observed
\widehat{r}=1
in
78\%
of the replicates and
\widehat{r}=3
in
22\%
of the replicates, while with
n=2000
and
\rho_{n}=0.4
we observed
\widehat{r}=3
in all replicates. The results in
Tables

2
and
1
indicate that our test statistic has well-controlled Type I error under the null and exhibits significant power under the alternative. Finally, recorded values of
\widehat{r}
also suggest that, although both the Laplace and Gaussian kernels are of infinite rank, we only need embedding dimensions of
\widehat{r}\leq 6
to obtain a valid and consistent test procedure. This supports the folklore observation in Statistics and Data Science that, for many inference tasks, high or even full rank models can be well-approximated by their low-rank counterparts.

Appendix A
Proofs of stated results

A.1
Proof of
Theorem

1
(positive semidefinite kernel
\kappa
)

Recall that by convention, we index the eigenvalues of
A
and
P
in decreasing order by magnitude. The eigenvalues of
P
are all non-negative by the positive semidefiniteness of
\kappa
, so in particular

|\widehat{\lambda}_{1}|\geq|\widehat{\lambda}_{2}|\geq\dots\geq|\widehat{\lambda}_{n}|,\quad\text{and}\quad\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{2}\geq\dots\geq\lambda_{n}.

For any
s\geq 1
, define the eigenvalue gap
\delta_{s}=\lambda_{s}-\lambda_{s+1}\geq 0
. Let
\mathcal{E}_{0}
be the event

\mathcal{E}_{0}=\left\{\delta_{r}\geq\max\{4\|E\|,8C_{2}(\nu)\log n\}\right\},

(A.1)

where
C_{2}(\nu)=\tfrac{2}{3}(\nu+2)
. By Weyl’s inequality and the stated hypotheses, on
\mathcal{E}_{0}
it holds that

\widehat{\lambda}_{r}\geq\lambda_{r}-\|E\|\geq\lambda_{r}-\frac{1}{4}\delta_{r}\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{r}>0.

(A.2)

Furthermore, by the discussion in the main text, since
\kappa
is positive semidefinite, for any
r\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\|U\Lambda^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2},\quad\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2},\quad\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}\rho_{n}^{1/2}.

(A.3)

Now, letting
W^{(n)}=\operatorname{arg~min}_{W\in\mathcal{O}(r)}\|\widehat{U}-UW\|_{F}
, consider the expansion

\begin{split}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}-U\Lambda^{1/2}W^{(n)}=(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}&+UU^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}-U\Lambda^{1/2}U^{\top}\widehat{U}\\
&+U\Lambda^{1/2}(U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)}).\end{split}

(A.4)

Next, we shall bound the two-to-infinity norm of each term on the right hand side of
Eq.

A.4
. Our bounds involve the following quantities that are well-controlled using the technical lemmas in
Appendix

B
. Specifically,

\displaystyle\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|\leq\frac{2\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}=\psi_{0},\qquad\psi_{1}=\|U^{\top}EU\|,

\displaystyle\psi_{2}=\|U^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}-\Lambda^{1/2}U^{\top}\widehat{U}\|,\qquad\psi_{3}^{(k)}=\|U_{\perp}|\Lambda_{\perp}|^{k}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU|\Lambda|^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}.

Bounding
R_{0}\coloneqq U\Lambda^{1/2}(U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)})

Applying
Eq.

1.1
and the aforementioned technical lemma gives

\begin{split}\|R_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}&=\|U\Lambda^{1/2}(U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)})\|_{2\to\infty}\\
&\leq\|U\Lambda^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)}\|\\
&\leq\|U\Lambda^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|^{2}\\
&\leq\|U\Lambda^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\psi_{0}^{2},\end{split}

(A.5)

where the second inequality follows from
[
15
, Lemma 6.7]
.

Bounding
R_{1}\coloneqq UU^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}-U\Lambda^{1/2}U^{\top}\widehat{U}

We have

\|R_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|\Lambda^{1/2}U^{\top}\widehat{U}-U^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}\|\leq\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\times\psi_{2},

where
\psi_{2}
is the upper bound for
\|\Lambda^{1/2}U^{\top}\widehat{U}-U^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}\|
given in
Lemma

6
and mentioned above (with an immaterial sign change inside the operator norm).

Bounding
(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}

By expanding
\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}=A\widehat{U}=P\widehat{U}+E\widehat{U}
, we have

\begin{split}(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}&=(I-UU^{\top})E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2}+(I-UU^{\top})P\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2}\\
&=E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2}-UU^{\top}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2}+(I-UU^{\top})P\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2}.\end{split}

The term
UU^{\top}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2}
admits the bound

\begin{split}\|UU^{\top}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|U^{\top}E\widehat{U}\|\times\|\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2}\|\\
&\leq\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\times\frac{1}{|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{1/2}}\times(\|U^{\top}EU\|+\|U^{\top}E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|)\\
&\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}(\psi_{1}+\|E\|\psi_{0})}{\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}.\end{split}

Above, the final inequality follows from bounding
\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|
via the Davis–Kahan theorem
[
23
]
and using
Eq.

A.2
to replace
\widehat{\lambda}_{r}
by
\lambda_{r}
.

Next, write
\Pi_{U}^{\perp}=I-UU^{\top}
. Repeatedly expanding
\widehat{U}
as
P\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}+E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}
yields

\begin{split}(I-UU^{\top})P\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2}&=\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{2}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-3/2}+\Pi_{U}^{\perp}PE\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-3/2}\\
&=\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{3}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-5/2}+\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{2}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-5/2}+\Pi_{U}^{\perp}PE\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-3/2}\\
&=\dots\\
&=\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{m}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(m-1/2)}+\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{k}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1/2)},\end{split}

which holds for any integer
m\geq 2
. For the first term on the right-hand side, we have

\begin{split}\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{m}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(m-1/2)}\|_{2\to\infty}&=\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{m}U_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(m-1/2)}\|_{2\to\infty}\\
&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|\Lambda_{\perp}\|^{m-1/2}\times\|U_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{U}\|\times\|\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(m-1/2)}\|\\
&\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}\times\psi_{0}\times\left|\frac{\lambda_{r+1}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{r}}\right|^{m-1/2},\end{split}

where the second inequality follows from bounding
\|U_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{U}\|
using the Davis–Kahan theorem, i.e.,
\|U_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{U}\|=\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|\leq\psi_{0}
.

On the event
\mathcal{E}_{0}
, it holds that

\frac{\lambda_{r+1}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{r}}\leq\frac{\lambda_{r+1}}{\lambda_{r}-\|E\|}\leq\frac{\lambda_{r+1}+\|E\|}{\lambda_{r}}\leq 1-\frac{\lambda_{r}-\lambda_{r+1}}{2\lambda_{r}}=1-\frac{\delta_{r}}{2\lambda_{r}}.

(A.6)

Since
\delta_{r}\geq 4\varsigma(\nu,n)\geq 18(n\rho_{n})^{1/2}
(see
Eq.

3.1
) and
\lambda_{r}\leq n\rho_{n}
, choosing

m=n\rho_{n}^{1/2}+1
yields

\left(1-\frac{\lambda_{r}-\lambda_{r-1}}{2\lambda_{r}}\right)^{m-1/2}\leq\left(1-\frac{9}{(n\rho_{n})^{1/2}}\right)^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\leq\exp\left(-9n^{1/2}\right),

(A.7)

where the final inequality holds since
1-x\leq e^{-x}
for
x\in[0,1]
. For simplicity of notations, we have assumed that
n\rho_{n}^{1/2}
is an integer, for otherwise we can replace it with
\lceil n\rho_{n}^{1/2}\rceil
without changing the subsequent argument. In summary,

\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{m}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(m-1/2)}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}\psi_{0}\exp(-9n^{1/2}).

(A.8)

We now evaluate
\zeta_{k}=\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{k}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1/2)}\|_{2\to\infty}
for
k\geq 1
.
Since
I=UU^{\top}+U_{\perp}U_{\perp}^{\top}
, we first write

\begin{split}\zeta_{k}&\leq\underbrace{\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{k}EUU^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1/2)}\|_{2\to\infty}}_{\zeta_{k}^{(1)}}+\underbrace{\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{k}EU_{\perp}U_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1/2)}\|_{2\to\infty}}_{\zeta_{k}^{(2)}}.\end{split}

For
\zeta_{k}^{(2)}
, observe that

\begin{split}\zeta_{k}^{(2)}&=\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{k}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU_{\perp}U_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1/2)}\|_{2\to\infty}\\
&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|\Lambda_{\perp}\|^{k-1/2}\times\|E\|\times\|U_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{U}\|\times\|\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}\|^{(k+1/2)}\\
&\leq\frac{\rho_{n}^{1/2}\|E\|\psi_{0}}{|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|}\times\left|\frac{\lambda_{r+1}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{r}}\right|^{k-1/2}\\
&\leq\frac{2\rho_{n}^{1/2}\|E\|\psi_{0}}{\lambda_{r}}\times\left(1-\frac{\delta_{r}}{2\lambda_{r}}\right)^{k-1/2},\end{split}

where the final inequality follows from
Eq.

A.6
. This in turn implies

\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\zeta_{k}^{(2)}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\frac{2\rho_{n}^{1/2}\|E\|\psi_{0}}{\lambda_{r}}\left(1-\frac{\delta_{r}}{2\lambda_{r}}\right)^{k-1/2}\leq\frac{4\rho_{n}^{1/2}\|E\|\psi_{0}}{\delta_{r}}.

Next, for
1\leq k\leq m
, it holds that

\begin{split}\zeta_{k}^{(1)}&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{k}U_{\perp}EU\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1/2)}\|\leq\psi_{3}^{(k)}|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{-(k+1/2)},\end{split}

where
\psi_{3}^{(k)}
is an upper bound for
\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{k}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU\|_{2\to\infty}
as given in
Lemma

7
, and we have used the fact that
\|U^{\top}\widehat{U}\|\leq 1
.

In summary, thus far, we have established that

(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}=E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2}+R_{2},

(A.9)

where
R_{2}
satisfies

\begin{split}\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}(\psi_{1}+\|E\|\psi_{0})}{\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}+\rho_{n}^{1/2}\psi_{0}\left(\exp(-9n^{1/2})+\frac{4\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}\right)\\
&\qquad+\sum_{k=1}^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\psi_{3}^{(k)}|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{-(k+1/2)}.\end{split}

(A.10)

Bounding
E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2}

We begin with the expansion

\begin{split}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2}&=EUU^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2}+E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2}\\
&=EU\Lambda^{-1/2}\left[W^{(n)}+(U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)})+(\Lambda^{1/2}U^{\top}\widehat{U}-U^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2})\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2}\right]\\
&\qquad+E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2},\end{split}

where
W^{(n)}
denotes the orthogonal matrix mentioned above that solves the Frobenius norm Procrustes problem for
U
and
\widehat{U}
. Considering the above expansion, we have

\displaystyle\|EU\Lambda^{-1/2}(U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)})\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|EU\Lambda^{-1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\psi_{0}^{2},

\displaystyle\|EU\Lambda^{-1/2}(\Lambda^{1/2}U^{\top}\widehat{U}-U^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2})\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}\|EU\Lambda^{-1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\psi_{2},

where
\psi_{2}
is an upper bound for
\|\Lambda^{1/2}U^{\top}\widehat{U}-U^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}\|
given in
Lemma

6
.

Our last remaining technical challenge in this proof is to bound

\|E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}
, and we do so through a
careful leave-one-out analysis. Leave-one-out style arguments provide a useful
and elegant approach for handling the (often times) complicated dependencies
between the rows of
\widehat{U}
. See

[
1
,
20
,
39
,
35
,
65
,
60
]

for various examples of leave-one-out analysis in the context of random graph
inference, linear regression using LASSO, and phase synchronization.

Although we are motivated in part by the ideas used in the proofs of
[
1
, Theorem 2.1]
and
[
39
, Theorem 3.1]
, our arguments require noticeably more delicate analysis in order to decompose the bound for
E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}
into a main order term
EU\Lambda^{-1/2}
and a negligible lower order term. Inference results in
Section

4.1
and
4.2
are subsequently made possible by further study of the main order term.

To proceed with the proof, we first introduce some helpful notations. Given
A
, define the collection of auxiliary matrices
A^{[1]},A^{[2]},\dots,A^{[n]}
, where for each
1\leq h\leq n
,

A^{[h]}(i,j)=\begin{cases}A(i,j)&\text{if $i\not=h$ and $j\not=h$},\\
P(i,j)&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(A.11)

Eq.

A.11
implies that the entries of
A^{[h]}-A
are all zeros except for the entries in the
h
-th row and
h
-th column which are the same as those for the
h
-th row and
h
-th column of
E=A-P
, up to a negative sign. For consistency of notation, let
\widehat{U}^{[h]}
be the
n\times r
matrix whose columns are the leading eigenvectors of
A^{[h]}
.

Lemma 2
.

Consider the setting in
Theorem

1
or
Theorem

2
. Define
\widehat{U}^{[h]}
as above, and let
V^{[h]}=(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}^{[h]}
. Suppose there exists
\nu>0
such that the events

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{0}=\left\{\delta_{r}\geq\max\{4\|E\|,8C_{2}(\nu)\log n\}\right\},

(A.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}=\left\{\forall h\in[n],\,\,\,\|e_{h}^{\top}EV^{[h]}\|\leq C_{1}(\nu)\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n}\|V^{[h]}\|_{F}+C_{2}(\nu)\|V^{[h]}\|_{2\to\infty}\log n\right\}

(A.13)

hold simultaneously, with
C_{1}(\nu)=\sqrt{2(\nu+2)}
and
C_{2}(\nu)=\tfrac{2}{3}(\nu+2)
. Then,

\begin{split}\|E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\frac{16C_{1}(\nu)\sqrt{r\rho_{n}\log n}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{8(\|E\|\cdot\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|EU\|_{2\to\infty})(C_{2}(\nu)\log n+2\|E\|)}{\delta_{r}}\\
&\qquad+\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\left(6C_{2}(\nu)\log n+\frac{16\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}}\right).\end{split}

(A.14)

We emphasize that, conditional on
P
,
V^{[h]}
is independent of the entries in the
h
-th row and
h
-th column of
E
. Therefore,
e_{h}^{\top}E
and
V^{[h]}
are independent, and we can bound the probability of the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
via
Lemma

8
.

By applying
Lemma

2
, we have

\begin{split}&\|E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\\
&\qquad\leq\frac{16\sqrt{2}C_{1}(\nu)\sqrt{r\rho_{n}\log n}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}\\
&\qquad\qquad+\frac{8\sqrt{2}(\|E\|\cdot\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|EU\|_{2\to\infty})(C_{2}(\nu)\log n+2\|E\|)}{\delta_{r}\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}\\
&\qquad\qquad+\sqrt{2}\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\left(6C_{2}(\nu)\log n+\frac{16\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}}\right).\end{split}

Next, observe that

\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\sqrt{2}\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty},

where the term
(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}
previously appeared in
Eq.

A.9
. Hence, we have

\begin{split}&\|E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\\
&\qquad\leq\frac{16\sqrt{2}C_{1}(\nu)\sqrt{r\rho_{n}\log n}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}\\
&\qquad\qquad+\frac{8\sqrt{2}(\|E\|\cdot\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|EU\|_{2\to\infty})(C_{2}(\nu)\log n+2\|E\|)}{\delta_{r}\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}\\
&\qquad\qquad+\lambda_{r}^{-1}\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\left(12C_{2}(\nu)\log n+\frac{32\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}}\right).\end{split}

Let
T_{*}=EU\Lambda^{-1/2}W^{(n)}
. Combining the above expressions, we obtain

\displaystyle(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=T_{*}+R_{2}+Y_{0}+Y_{1}+Y_{2},

\displaystyle\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|T_{*}\|_{2\to\infty}(\psi_{0}^{2}+2^{1/2}\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}\psi_{2}),

\displaystyle\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16\sqrt{2}C_{1}(\nu)\sqrt{r\rho_{n}\log n}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{8\sqrt{2}(\|E\|\cdot\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|EU\|_{2\to\infty})(C_{2}(\nu)\log n+2\|E\|)}{\delta_{r}\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\|Y_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{12C_{2}(\nu)\log n+32\delta_{r}^{-1}\|E\|^{2}}{\lambda_{r}}\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}.

Hence, provided
\lambda_{r}\geq 24C_{2}(\nu)\log n+64\delta_{r}^{-1}\|E\|^{2}
, then

\displaystyle\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{1-\frac{12C_{2}(\nu)\log n+32\delta_{r}^{-1}\|E\|^{2}}{\lambda_{r}}}\left(\|T_{*}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\right)

and

\displaystyle\|Y_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{24C_{2}(\nu)\log n+64\delta_{r}^{-1}\|E\|^{2}}{\lambda_{r}}\left(\|T_{*}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\right).

A.1.1
Wrapping up

In summary, suppose there exists a constant
\nu>0
such that all of the following events simultaneously hold.

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{0}=\{\delta_{r}\geq\max\{6\|E\|,8C_{2}(\nu)\log n\}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}=\{\forall h\in[n],\,\,\,\|e_{h}^{\top}EV^{[h]}\|\leq C_{1}(\nu)\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n}\|V^{[h]}\|_{F}+C_{2}(\nu)\|V^{[h]}\|_{2\to\infty}\log n\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}=\{\lambda_{r}\geq 24C_{2}(\nu)\log n+64\delta_{r}^{-1}\|E\|^{2})\}.

Recall that
V^{[h]}=(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}^{[h]}
,
C_{1}(\nu)=\sqrt{2(\nu+2)}
, and
C_{2}(\nu)=\tfrac{2}{3}(\nu+2)
.

We therefore have

\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}-U\Lambda^{1/2}W^{(n)}=EU\Lambda^{-1/2}W^{(n)}+R_{0}+R_{1}+R_{2}+Y_{0}+Y_{1},

(A.15)

where
T_{*}=EU\Lambda^{-1/2}W^{(n)}
and the residuals
R_{0}
through
Y_{2}
satisfy

\displaystyle\|R_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|U\Lambda^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\psi_{0}^{2},\hskip 17.00024pt\|R_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\psi_{2}

\displaystyle\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}(\psi_{1}+\|E\|\psi_{0})}{\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}+\rho_{n}^{1/2}\psi_{0}\left(\exp(-9n^{1/2})+\frac{4\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}\right)+\sum_{k=1}^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\psi_{3}^{(k)}|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{-(k+1/2)},

\displaystyle\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|T_{*}\|_{2\to\infty}(\psi_{0}^{2}+2^{1/2}\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}\psi_{2}),

\displaystyle\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\frac{16\sqrt{2}C_{1}(\nu)\sqrt{r\rho_{n}\log n}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}+\frac{8\sqrt{2}(\|E\|\cdot\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|EU\|_{2\to\infty})(C_{2}(\nu)\log n+2\|E\|)}{\delta_{r}\lambda_{r}^{1/2}},

\displaystyle\|Y_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\frac{24C_{2}(\nu)+64\delta_{r}^{-1}\|E\|^{2})}{\lambda_{r}}\left(\|T_{*}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\right).

We now bound each term in the above display equations. By
Lemma

4
,

\|E\|\leq\varsigma(\nu,n)

(A.16)

with probability at least
1-n^{-\nu}
, and hence

\psi_{0}=\frac{2\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}\leq\frac{2\varsigma(\nu,n)}{\delta_{r}}

with probability at least
1-n^{-\nu}
. Next, for
\psi_{1}
, we have by
Lemma

5
that

\psi_{1}=\|U^{\top}EU\|\leq 4\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}+\frac{8}{3}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)

with probability at least
1-2n^{-\nu}
, where
\vartheta(\nu,r,n)=\nu\log n+r\log 9
.
In the positive semidefinite setting, we have
\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}
so that

\psi_{1}\leq 4\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}+\frac{8}{3}\lambda_{r}^{-1}\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)

with probability at least
1-2n^{-\nu}
.
For
\psi_{2}
, by
Lemma

6
,

\psi_{2}\leq\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}\left(4\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}+\frac{8}{3}\lambda_{r}^{-1}\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)+\frac{2\varsigma(\nu,n)^{2}}{\delta_{r}}\right)

with probability at least
1-2n^{-\nu}
.
For
\psi_{3}^{(k)}
, by
Lemma

7
,

\begin{split}\psi_{3}^{(k)}&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{k}\|_{2\to\infty}\left(\frac{8\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)}{3}+\sqrt{8\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)}\right)\\
&\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}\lambda_{r+1}^{k-1/2}\left(\frac{8\rho_{n}^{1/2}\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)}{3\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}+\sqrt{8\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)}\right)\end{split}

with probability at least
1-n^{-(\nu+1)}
.
The above derivations use the fact that in the positive semidefinite setting,

\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}\lambda_{r}^{-1/2},\quad\text{and}\quad\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}.

Taking a union over all
k
satisfying
1\leq k\leq n\rho_{n}^{1/2}
, we obtain

\begin{split}\sum_{k=1}^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\psi_{3}^{(k)}|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{-(k+1/2)}&\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{-1}\left(\frac{8\rho_{n}^{1/2}\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)}{3\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}+\sqrt{8\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)}\right)\sum_{k=1}^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\left|\frac{\lambda_{r+1}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{r}}\right|^{k-1/2}\\
&\leq\frac{2\rho_{n}}{\lambda_{r}}\Bigl(\frac{8\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)}{3\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}+\sqrt{8\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)}\Bigr)\sum_{k=1}^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\Bigl(1-\frac{\delta_{r}}{2\lambda_{r}}\Bigr)^{k-1/2}\\
&\leq\frac{4\rho_{n}}{\delta_{r}}\Bigl(\frac{8\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)}{3\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}+\sqrt{8\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)}\Bigr)\end{split}

with probability at least
1-n^{-\nu}
, where the second inequality in the above display follows from
Eq.

A.6
.
Next, by
Lemma

8
,

\begin{split}\|EU\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\sqrt{8\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu+1,r,n)}+\tfrac{8}{3}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\vartheta(\nu+1,r,n)\\
&\leq\frac{11}{2}\rho_{n}^{1/2}\sqrt{\vartheta(\nu+1,r,n)}\end{split}

(A.17)

with probability at least
1-n^{-\nu}
, where the final inequality follows from the fact that
\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}
and
\lambda_{r}\geq\vartheta(\nu+1,r,n)
.

Using the same argument as before, we also have

\begin{split}\|EU\Lambda^{-1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\sqrt{8\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu+1,r,n)}\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}+\tfrac{8}{3}\|U\Lambda^{-1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\vartheta(\nu+1,r,n)\\
&\leq\frac{11}{2}\rho_{n}^{1/2}\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\vartheta(\nu+1,r,n)},\end{split}

(A.18)

where now
\|U\Lambda^{-1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}\lambda_{r}^{-1}
.
Eq.

A.18
implies the bound in
Eq.

3.6
.

Finally, we bound the probability of the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
in
Lemma

2
. Recall that
V^{[h]}_{i}
denotes the
i
-th row of
V^{[h]}=(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}^{[h]}
and that
V^{[h]}
is independent of
e_{h}^{\top}E
as
\widehat{U}^{[h]}
is a function of the entries in
A^{[h]}
which are mutually independent from the entries in the
h
-th row of
E
. Therefore, by the matrix Bernstein inequality (e.g., see
[
54
, Theorem 1.6]
), we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\|e_{h}^{\top}EV^{[h]}\|\geq t\right)\leq(r+1)\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}/2}{\sigma_{h}^{2}+\|V^{[h]}\|_{2\to\infty}t/3}\right),

where
\sigma_{h}^{2}=\sum_{j}P(i,j)(1-P(i,j))\|V^{[h]}_{i}\|^{2}\leq\rho_{n}\|V^{[h]}\|_{F}^{2}
.
We thus have

\|e_{h}^{\top}EV^{[h]}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\sqrt{2(\nu+2)\rho_{n}\log n}\|V^{[h]}\|_{F}+\frac{2(\nu+2)}{3}\|V^{[h]}\|_{2\to\infty}\log n

(A.19)

with probability at least
1-2n^{-(\nu+1)}
. By taking a union over all
h\in[n]
, we conclude that
\mathcal{E}_{1}
holds with probability at least
1-2n^{-\nu}
, where
C_{1}(\nu)=\sqrt{2(\nu+2)}
and
C_{2}(\nu)=\tfrac{2}{3}(\nu+2)
.

For ease of presentation, we shall now drop explicit constants from our derivations. Readers who are interested in keeping track of these constants can continue to do so using the values given above. By combining the above bounds and noting that
\vartheta(\nu,n)\lesssim(n\rho_{n})^{1/2}
, after some tedious algebraic manipulations, we have

\displaystyle\|R_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}\lesssim\|U\Lambda^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\frac{n\rho_{n}}{\delta_{r}^{2}},

\displaystyle\|R_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\lesssim\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\left(\frac{\rho_{n}^{1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}+\frac{n\rho_{n}}{\delta_{r}\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}\right),

\displaystyle\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}\lesssim\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\left(\frac{\rho_{n}^{1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}+\frac{n\rho_{n}}{\delta_{r}\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}\right)+\frac{n\rho_{n}^{3/2}}{\delta_{r}^{2}}+\frac{\rho_{n}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{\delta_{r}},

\displaystyle\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}\lesssim\frac{\rho_{n}^{1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}\left(\frac{n\rho_{n}}{\delta_{r}^{2}}+\frac{\rho_{n}^{1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{\lambda_{r}}\right),

\displaystyle\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\frac{(r\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}\times(n\rho_{n})^{1/2}}{\delta_{r}\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}+\frac{\rho_{n}^{1/2}\log^{3/2}{n}}{\lambda_{r}^{1/2}\delta_{r}}+\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\times\frac{n\rho_{n}}{\delta_{r}\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-\nu})
. Substituting these expressions into
Eq.

A.15
and using the fact that
\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}
and
\|U\Lambda^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}
, we obtain

\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}-U\Lambda^{1/2}W^{(n)}=EU\Lambda^{-1/2}W^{(n)}+\widetilde{R},

where
\widetilde{R}
satisfies

\begin{split}\|\widetilde{R}\|_{2\to\infty}&\lesssim\frac{n\rho_{n}^{3/2}}{\delta_{r}^{2}}+\frac{(\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}}{\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}\left(\frac{(rn\rho_{n})^{1/2}+\log n}{\delta_{r}}\right)\end{split}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-\nu})
. The stated result follows from multiplying both side of the above display by
(W^{(n)})^{\top}
and then redefining (rewriting) this matrix without the transpose operation. Note that the two-to-infinity norm is right-orthogonal invariant, i.e.,
\|MW\|_{2\to\infty}=\|M\|_{2\to\infty}
for any
n\times r
matrix
M
and
r\times r
orthogonal matrix
W
. This concludes the proof of
Theorem

1
.

A.2
Proof of
Lemma

2

Let
e_{h}
denote the
h
-th elementary basis vector in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For each
h\in[n]
, consider the expansion

\begin{split}e_{h}^{\top}E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}&=e_{h}^{\top}E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}^{[h]}\widehat{U}^{[h]\top}\widehat{U}+e_{h}^{\top}E(I-\widehat{U}^{[h]}\widehat{U}^{[h]\top})\widehat{U}\\
&\qquad-e_{h}^{\top}EUU^{\top}(I-\widehat{U}^{[h]}\widehat{U}^{[h]\top})\widehat{U}.\end{split}

(A.20)

By submultiplicativity of the operator norm, together with the fact that
\|UU^{\top}\|=1
and
\|e_{h}\|=1
for each
h
, we have

\displaystyle\|e_{h}^{\top}EUU^{\top}(I-\widehat{U}^{[h]}\widehat{U}^{[h]\top})\widehat{U}\|\leq\|E\|\times\|(I-\widehat{U}^{[h]}\widehat{U}^{[h]\top})\widehat{U}\|,

\displaystyle\|e_{h}^{\top}E(I-\widehat{U}^{[h]}\widehat{U}^{[h]\top})\widehat{U}\|\leq\|E\|\times\|(I-\widehat{U}^{[h]}\widehat{U}^{[h]\top})\widehat{U}\|.

Let
\widehat{\lambda}_{r}^{[h]}
denote the
r
-th largest eigenvalue of
A^{[h]}
. Under the event
\mathcal{E}_{0}
specified in
Eq.

A.1
, we have by Weyl’s inequality that

\widehat{\lambda}_{r}^{[h]}-\widehat{\lambda}_{r+1}\geq\lambda_{r}-\|E\|-\widehat{\lambda}_{r+1}\geq\lambda_{r}-2\|E\|-\lambda_{r+1}\geq\frac{1}{2}\delta_{r}.

So, by applying the general form of the Davis–Kahan theorem (e.g., see
[
7
, Theorem VII.3.1]
), we have

\begin{split}\|(I-\widehat{U}^{[h]}\widehat{U}^{[h]\top})\widehat{U}\|&\leq\frac{\|(A^{[h]}-A)\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|}{\widehat{\lambda}_{r}^{[h]}-\widehat{\lambda}_{r+1}}\leq\frac{2\|(A^{[h]}-A)\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|}{\delta_{r}}.\end{split}

(A.21)

For the numerator term, by the construction of
A^{[h]}
, we have

\begin{split}\|(A^{[h]}-A)\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|&\leq\|\operatorname{diag}(E_{1,h},\dots,E_{n,h})1_{n\times 1}\widehat{U}^{[h]}_{h}\|+\|e_{h}^{\top}E\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|\\
&=\|(E_{1,h},\dots,E_{n,h})^{\top}\|\times\|\widehat{U}^{[h]}_{h}\|+\|e_{h}^{\top}E\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|\\
&\leq\|E\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|e_{h}^{\top}E\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|\\
&\leq\|E\|\times\|\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|e_{h}^{\top}E\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|.\end{split}

(A.22)

Next, since
\|U^{\top}\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|\leq 1
and
\|\widehat{U}^{\top}\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|\leq 1
, it follows that

\begin{split}\|\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\|UU^{\top}\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|(I-UU)^{\top}\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|_{2\to\infty}\\
&\leq\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|(I-UU^{\top})[\widehat{U}\widehat{U}^{\top}\widehat{U}^{[h]}+(I-\widehat{U}\widehat{U}^{\top})\widehat{U}^{[h]}]\|_{2\to\infty}\\
&\leq\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|(I-\widehat{U}\widehat{U}^{\top})\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|.\end{split}

(A.23)

Here, properties of the
\sin

\Theta
distance (e.g., see
[
13
, Lemma 1]
) yield

\|(I-\widehat{U}\widehat{U}^{\top})\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|=\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{U},\widehat{U}^{[h]})\|=\|(I-\widehat{U}^{[h]}\widehat{U}^{[h]\top})\widehat{U}\|.

(A.24)

Hence, by substituting
Eq.

A.23
into
Eq.

A.22
, we obtain

\begin{split}\|(A^{[h]}-A)\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|&\leq\|E\|(\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty})\\
&\qquad+\|E\|\times\|(I-\widehat{U}^{[h]}\widehat{U}^{[h]\top})\widehat{U}\|+\|e_{h}^{\top}E\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|.\end{split}

(A.25)

Substituting
Eq.

A.25
into
Eq.

A.21
and then rearranging terms yields

\|(I-\widehat{U}^{[h]}\widehat{U}^{[h]\top})\widehat{U}\|\leq\frac{4\|E\|(\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty})+4\|e_{h}^{\top}E\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|}{\delta_{r}}

(A.26)

under the condition
\delta_{r}\geq 4\|E\|
implied by the event
\mathcal{E}_{0}
.

Writing

E\widehat{U}^{[h]}=EUU^{\top}\widehat{U}^{[h]}+EV^{[h]}
leads to

\begin{split}\|e_{h}^{\top}E\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|&\leq\|e_{h}^{\top}EUU^{\top}\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|+\|e_{h}^{\top}EV^{[h]}\|\leq\|EU\|_{2\to\infty}+\|e_{h}^{\top}EV^{[h]}\|,\end{split}

(A.27)

and hence

\begin{split}\|(I-\widehat{U}^{[h]}\widehat{U}^{[h]\top})\widehat{U}\|&\leq\frac{4\|E\|(\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty})}{\delta_{r}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{4\|EU\|_{2\to\infty}+4\|e_{h}^{\top}EV^{[h]}\|}{\delta_{r}}.\end{split}

(A.28)

Now, recall the definition of the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
in
Eq.

A.13
, and note that

\|V^{[h]}\|_{F}=\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{U}^{[h]},U)\|_{F}\leq\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{U}^{[h]},\widehat{U})\|_{F}+\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{U},U)\|_{F}\leq\frac{4r^{1/2}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}.

Furthermore,

\begin{split}\|V^{[h]}\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{U}^{\top}\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|(I-UU^{\top})(I-\widehat{U}\widehat{U}^{\top})\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|_{2\to\infty}\\
&\leq\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|(I-\widehat{U}\widehat{U}^{\top})\widehat{U}^{[h]}\|\\
&\leq\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|(I-\widehat{U}^{[h]}\widehat{U}^{[h]\top})\widehat{U}\|,\end{split}

where the final inequality follows from
Eq.

A.24
.

Substituting the above expressions for
\|V^{[h]}\|_{F}
and
\|V^{[h]}\|_{2\to\infty}
into
Eq.

A.13
yields

\begin{split}\|e_{h}^{\top}EV^{[h]}\|&\leq\frac{4C_{1}(\nu)\sqrt{r\rho_{n}\log n}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}\\
&\qquad+C_{2}(\nu)(\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|(I-\widehat{U}^{[h]}\widehat{U}^{[h]\top})\widehat{U}\|)\log n.\end{split}

(A.29)

Substituting
Eq.

A.29
into
Eq.

A.28
and then rearranging terms yields

\begin{split}\|(I-\widehat{U}^{[h]}\widehat{U}^{[h]\top})\widehat{U}\|&\leq\frac{8\|E\|(\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\ \to\infty})}{\delta_{r}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{8\|EU\|_{2\to\infty}}{\delta_{r}}+\frac{32C_{1}(\nu)\sqrt{r\rho_{n}\log n}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}^{2}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{8C_{2}(\nu)\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\log n}{\delta_{r}},\end{split}

(A.30)

provided that
\delta_{r}\geq 8C_{2}(\nu)\log n
. Another substitution of the above into
Eq.

A.29
yields

\begin{split}\|e_{h}^{\top}EV^{[h]}\|&\leq\frac{4C_{1}(\nu)\sqrt{r\rho_{n}\log n}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}+C_{2}(\nu)\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\log n\\
&\qquad+\frac{8C_{2}(\nu)\|E\|(\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\ \to\infty})\log n}{\delta_{r}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{8C_{2}(\nu)\|EU\|_{2\to\infty}\log n}{\delta_{r}}+\frac{32C_{1}(\nu)C_{2}(\nu)\sqrt{r\rho_{n}\log^{3}{n}}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}^{2}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{8C_{2}(\nu)^{2}\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\log^{2}{n}}{\delta_{r}}.\end{split}

(A.31)

Finally, substituting
Eq.

A.31
and
Eq.

A.30
into
Eq.

A.20
yields

\begin{split}\|e_{h}^{\top}E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|&\leq\frac{4C_{1}(\nu)\sqrt{r\rho_{n}\log n}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}+C_{2}(\nu)\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\log n\\
&\qquad+\frac{8C_{2}(\nu)\|E\|(\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\ \to\infty})\log n}{\delta_{r}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{8C_{2}(\nu)\|EU\|_{2\to\infty}\log n}{\delta_{r}}+\frac{32C_{1}(\nu)C_{2}(\nu)\sqrt{r\rho_{n}\log^{3}{n}}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}^{2}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{8C_{2}(\nu)^{2}\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\log^{2}{n}}{\delta_{r}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{16\|E\|^{2}(\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\ \to\infty})}{\delta_{r}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{16\|E\|\cdot\|EU\|_{2\to\infty}}{\delta_{r}}+\frac{32C_{1}(\nu)\sqrt{r\rho_{n}\log n}\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}^{2}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{8C_{2}(\nu)\|E\|\cdot\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\log n}{\delta_{r}}.\end{split}

Under the event
\mathcal{E}_{0}
, we can further obtain the simplification

\begin{split}\|e_{h}^{\top}E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|&\leq\frac{16C_{1}(\nu)\sqrt{r\rho_{n}\log n}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{8(\|E\|\cdot\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|EU\|_{2\to\infty})(C_{2}(\nu)\log n+2\|E\|)}{\delta_{r}}\\
&\qquad+\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\left(6C_{2}(\nu)\log n+\frac{16\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}}\right).\end{split}

(A.32)

Note that the right hand side of
Eq.

A.32
does not depend on
h
. In summary, we have shown

\begin{split}\|E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\frac{16C_{1}(\nu)\sqrt{r\rho_{n}\log n}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{8(\|E\|\cdot\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|EU\|_{2\to\infty})(C_{2}(\nu)\log n+2\|E\|)}{\delta_{r}}\\
&\qquad+\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\left(6C_{2}(\nu)\log n+\frac{16\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}}\right),\end{split}

(A.33)

as claimed. This completes the proof of
Lemma

2
.

A.3
Proof of
Theorem

2

In this proof we follow the same steps as in
Section

A.1
, with the main difference being that we have to avoid terms depending on

\|U_{\perp}|\Lambda_{\perp}|^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}

as we can no longer guarantee that
\|U_{\perp}|\Lambda_{\perp}|^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}
. Indeed, in general

\|U_{\perp}|\Lambda_{\perp}|^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}=\|U_{\perp}|\Lambda_{\perp}|U_{\perp}^{\top}\|_{\max},

and the entries of both
U_{\perp}|\Lambda_{\perp}|U_{\perp}^{\top}
and
|P|=(P^{2})^{1/2}
are not directly related to those of
P
. In other words, there is no closed form expression for
|P|
in terms of simple element-wise operations on
P
when
P
is indefinite. Similarly, we cannot guarantee that
\|U|\Lambda|^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}
.
Nevertheless, our approach involves the quantities

\displaystyle\epsilon_{0}\coloneqq\|U|\Lambda|^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|U\Lambda\|_{2\to\infty}\times\||\Lambda|^{-1/2}\|\leq n^{1/2}\rho_{n}\times|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2},

(A.34)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}\coloneqq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}=\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{2}U_{\perp}^{\top}\|_{\max}^{1/2}\leq n^{1/2}\rho_{n},

(A.35)

where the final inequalities above are due to the fact that
P^{2}
is positive semidefinite, so

\begin{split}\max\{\|U\Lambda\|_{2\to\infty}^{2},\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\}&=\max\{\|U\Lambda^{2}U^{\top}\|_{\max},\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{2}U_{\perp}^{\top}\|_{\max}\}\\
&\leq\|P^{2}\|_{\max}\leq n\|P\|_{\max}^{2}\leq n\rho_{n}^{2}.\end{split}

(A.36)

If
P
were positive semidefinite, then
Eq.

A.3
implies
\epsilon_{1}\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}\lambda_{r+1}^{1/2}
. The bounds in
Eqs.

A.34
and
A.35
are thus weaker that those in
Eq.

A.3
as
|\lambda_{r}/(n\rho_{n})|^{1/2}
is a decreasing function of
r
(for
n
fixed).

Recall that, by convention, we arrange the eigenvalues of
A
and
P
in decreasing order of magnitude, i.e.,

|\widehat{\lambda}_{1}|\geq|\widehat{\lambda}_{2}|\geq\dots\geq|\widehat{\lambda}_{n}|,\qquad\text{and}\qquad|\lambda_{1}|\geq|\lambda_{2}|\geq\dots\geq|\lambda_{n}|.

Once again, as in previous proofs, consider the event

\mathcal{E}_{0}=\{\delta_{r}\geq\max\{4\|E\|,8C_{2}(\nu)\log n\}\},

(A.37)

where
C_{2}(\nu)=\tfrac{2}{3}(\nu+2)
.
Now, consider

W^{(+)}=\argmin_{W\in\mathcal{O}_{r_{+}}}\|\widehat{U}_{+}-U_{+}W\|_{F},\quad W^{(-)}=\argmin_{W\in\mathcal{O}_{r_{-}}}\|\widehat{U}_{-}-U_{-}W\|_{F},

where
r_{+}
and
r_{-}
are the number of positive and negative eigenvalues among
\{\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{r}\}
, respectively, where
U_{+}
and
U_{-}
are the eigenvectors of
P
corresponding to the positive and negative eigenvalues among
\{\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{r}\}
, and where
\widehat{U}_{+}
and
\widehat{U}_{-}
are the eigenvectors of
A
corresponding to the positive and negative eigenvalues among
\{\widehat{\lambda}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\lambda}_{r}\}
.
Using these matrices, define the block-structured orthogonal matrix

W^{(n)}=\begin{bmatrix}W^{(+)}&0\\
0&W^{(-)}\end{bmatrix}.

Let
J
be the
r\times r
diagonal matrix with diagonal entries equal to
-1
or
1
such that
\Lambda=|\Lambda|J
. Note that, under
\mathcal{E}_{0}
, we also have
|\widehat{\Lambda}|=\widehat{\Lambda}J
. Furthermore,
JW^{(n)}=W^{(n)}J
. Since

U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)}=\begin{bmatrix}U_{+}^{\top}\widehat{U}_{+}-W^{(+)}&0\\
0&U_{-}^{\top}\widehat{U}_{-}-W^{(-)}\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}0&U_{+}^{\top}\widehat{U}_{-}\\
U_{-}^{\top}\widehat{U}_{+}&0\end{bmatrix},

it holds that

\|U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)}\|\leq\max\{\|U_{+}^{\top}\widehat{U}_{+}-W^{(+)}\|,\|U_{-}^{\top}\widehat{U}_{-}-W^{(-)}\|\}+\max\{\|U_{+}^{\top}\widehat{U}_{-}\|,\|U_{-}^{\top}\widehat{U}_{+}\|\}.

By
[
15
, Lemma 6.7]
and the Davis–Kahan theorem, we have the bound

\|U_{+}^{\top}\widehat{U}_{+}-W^{(+)}\|\leq\|(I-U_{+}U_{+}^{\top})\widehat{U}_{+})\|^{2}\leq\left(\frac{2\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}\right)^{2}=\psi_{0}^{2},

which likewise applies to
\|U_{-}^{\top}\widehat{U}_{-}-W^{(-)}\|
. Next, by the general form of the Davis–Kahan theorem
[
7
, Theorem VII.3.1]
, we obtain

\begin{split}\|U_{+}^{\top}\widehat{U}_{-}\|&\leq\frac{\|U_{+}^{\top}(A-P)\widehat{U}_{-}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}\\
&\leq\frac{\|U_{+}^{\top}EU_{-}\|+\|U_{+}^{\top}E(I-U_{-}U_{-}^{\top})\widehat{U}_{-}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}\\
&\leq\frac{\|U^{\top}EU\|+\|E\|\times\|(I-U_{-}U_{-}^{\top})\widehat{U}_{-}\|}{|\lambda_{r}|}\\
&\leq\frac{\psi_{1}+\|E\|\psi_{0}}{|\lambda_{r}|},\end{split}

and similarly for
\|U_{-}^{\top}\widehat{U}_{+}\|
. Combining the above bounds yields

\|U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)}\|\leq\psi_{0}^{2}+|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}(\psi_{1}+\|E\|\psi_{0}).

(A.38)

Now, consider the decomposition

\begin{split}\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}-U|\Lambda|^{1/2}W^{(n)}&=(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}\\
&\qquad+U(U^{\top}\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}J-|\Lambda|^{1/2}JU^{\top}\widehat{U})J\\
&\qquad+U|\Lambda|^{1/2}J(U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)})J,\end{split}

(A.39)

where we have used the fact that
JW^{(n)}J=W^{(n)}
.

For
R_{0}\coloneqq U|\Lambda|^{1/2}J(U^{\top}\widehat{U})-W^{(n)})J
, applying
Eq.

A.38
yields

\begin{split}\|U|\Lambda|^{1/2}J(U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)})J\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\|U|\Lambda|^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|J(U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)})J\|\\
&\leq\|U|\Lambda|^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)}\|\\
&\leq\epsilon_{0}(\psi_{0}^{2}+|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}\|E\|\psi_{0}+|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}\psi_{1}).\end{split}

Next, for
R_{1}\coloneqq U(U^{\top}\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}J-|\Lambda|^{1/2}JU^{\top}\widehat{U})J
, we have

\|R_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\times\||\Lambda|^{1/2}JU^{\top}\widehat{U}-U^{\top}\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}J\|\leq\epsilon_{0}|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}\psi_{2},

where
\psi_{2}
is the upper bound for
\||\Lambda|^{1/2}JU^{\top}\widehat{U}-U^{\top}\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}J\|
given in
Lemma

6
.

Bounding
(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}

Due to
\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|=\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}J=A\widehat{U}J=(P\widehat{U}+E\widehat{U})J
, we have

\begin{split}(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}&=(I-UU^{\top})E\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J+(I-UU^{\top})P\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J\\
&=E\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J-UU^{\top}E\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J+(I-UU^{\top})P\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J.\end{split}

The term
UU^{\top}E\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J
is bounded using the same argument as in the positive semidefinite case, namely

\begin{split}\|UU^{\top}E\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J\|_{2\to\infty}&=\|UU^{\top}E\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}\epsilon_{0}(\psi_{1}+\|E\|\psi_{0})}{|\lambda_{r}|}.\end{split}

Let
\Pi_{U}^{\perp}=(I-UU^{\top})
. Observe that the term
(I-UU^{\top})P\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J
admits the expansion

\begin{split}\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J&=(\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{2}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}+\Pi_{U}^{\perp}PE\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1})|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J\\
&=(\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{3}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}+\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{2}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}+\Pi_{U}^{\perp}PE\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1})|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J\\
&\dots\\
&=\left(\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{m}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(m-1)}+\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{k}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-k}\right)|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J.\end{split}

We then have

\begin{split}&\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{m}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(m-1)}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J\|_{2\to\infty}\\
&\qquad\qquad=\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{m}U_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-(m-1/2)}\|_{2\to\infty}\\
&\qquad\qquad\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|\Lambda_{\perp}\|^{m-1}\times\|U_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{U}\|\times\||\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-(m-1/2)}\|\\
&\qquad\qquad\leq\epsilon_{1}\times\psi_{0}\times|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{-1/2}\left|\frac{\lambda_{r+1}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{r}}\right|^{(m-1)}.\end{split}

Under
\mathcal{E}_{0}
, we have

\frac{|\lambda_{r+1}|}{|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|}\leq 1-\frac{|\lambda_{r}|-|\lambda_{r+1}|}{2|\lambda_{r}|}.

(A.40)

Setting
m=n\rho_{n}^{1/2}+1
, the same argument as that for
Eq.

A.8
yields

\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{m}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(m-1)}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\sqrt{2}\epsilon_{1}\psi_{0}|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}\exp(-9n^{1/2}).

(A.41)

We now evaluate
\zeta_{k}\coloneqq\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{k}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-k}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J\|_{2\to\infty}
for
k\geq 1
. We first write

\begin{split}\zeta_{k}&\leq\underbrace{\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{k}EUU^{\top}\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-(k+1/2)}\|_{2\to\infty}}_{\zeta_{k}^{(1)}}+\underbrace{\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{k}EU_{\perp}U_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-(k+1/2)}\|_{2\to\infty}}_{\zeta_{k}^{(2)}}.\end{split}

We then have

\begin{split}\zeta_{k}^{(2)}&=\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{k}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU_{\perp}U_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-(k+1/2)}\|_{2\to\infty}\\
&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|\Lambda_{\perp}\|^{k-1}\times\|E\|\times\|U_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{U}\|\times\|\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}\|^{(k+1/2)}\\
&\leq\frac{\epsilon_{1}\|E\|\psi_{0}}{|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{3/2}}\times\left|\frac{\lambda_{r+1}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{r}}\right|^{k-1}\\
&\leq\frac{2^{3/2}\epsilon_{1}\|E\|\psi_{0}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{3/2}}\times\left(1-\frac{|\lambda_{r}|-|\lambda_{r+1}|}{2|\lambda_{r}|}\right)^{k-1},\end{split}

and hence

\sum_{k=1}^{n^{1/2}\rho_{n}}\zeta_{k}^{(2)}\leq\frac{2^{3/2}\epsilon_{1}\|E\|\psi_{0}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{3/2}}\times\left(1-\frac{|\lambda_{r}|-|\lambda_{r+1}|}{2|\lambda_{r}|}\right)^{k-1}\leq\frac{2^{5/2}\epsilon_{1}\|E\|\psi_{0}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}\delta_{r}}.

(A.42)

Observe that we avoid directly bounding
\|U_{\perp}|\Lambda_{\perp}\|^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}
. For
\zeta_{k}^{(1)}
, we have

\begin{split}\zeta_{k}^{(1)}&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{k}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}\|^{(k+1/2)}\leq\psi_{3}^{(k)}\times|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{-(k+1/2)},\end{split}

where
\psi_{3}^{(k)}
is an upper bound for
\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{k}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU\|
given in
Lemma

7
.

In summary, we have

(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}=E\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J+R_{2},

where
R_{2}
satisfies

\begin{split}\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\frac{2^{1/2}\epsilon_{0}(\psi_{1}+\|E\|\psi_{0})}{|\lambda_{r}|}+\frac{\sqrt{2}\epsilon_{1}\psi_{0}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}\left(\exp(-18\sqrt{n})+\frac{4\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}\right)\\
&\qquad+\sum_{k=1}^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\psi_{3}^{(k)}|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{-(k+1/2)}.\end{split}

(A.43)

Bounding
E\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J

We once again have the expansion

\begin{split}E\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J&=EU|\Lambda|^{-1/2}J[W^{(n)}+(U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)})]\\
&\qquad+EU|\Lambda|^{-1/2}J(|\Lambda|^{1/2}JU^{\top}\widehat{U}-U^{\top}\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}J)|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J\\
&\qquad+E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J.\end{split}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|EU|\Lambda|^{-1/2}J(U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)})\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|EU|\Lambda|^{-1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times(\psi_{0}^{2}+|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}\|E\|\psi_{0}+|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}\psi_{1}),

\displaystyle\|EU|\Lambda|^{-1/2}(|\Lambda|^{1/2}JU^{\top}\widehat{U}-U^{\top}\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}J)|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J\|\leq\|EU|\Lambda|^{-1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times 2^{1/2}|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}\psi_{2},

where
\psi_{2}
is an upper bound for
\||\Lambda|^{1/2}JU^{\top}\widehat{U}-U^{\top}\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}J\|
given in
Lemma

6
, and we bounded
\|U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)}\|
using
Eq.

A.38
.

The term
\|E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J\|_{2\to\infty}=\|E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}
can be bounded using
Lemma

2
within the proof of
Theorem

1
. In summary, we have

\begin{split}&\|E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{-1/2}J\|_{2\to\infty}\\
&\qquad\qquad\leq\frac{16\sqrt{2}C_{1}(\nu)\sqrt{r\rho_{n}\log n}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{8\sqrt{2}(\|E\|\cdot\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|EU\|_{2\to\infty})(C_{2}(\nu)\log n+2\|E\|)}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\left(12C_{2}(\nu)\log n+\frac{32\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}}\right).\end{split}

Let
T_{*}=EU|\Lambda|^{-1/2}JW^{(n)}
. By combining the above expressions,

\displaystyle(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}

\displaystyle=T_{*}+R_{2}+R_{3}+Y_{0}+Y_{1},

\displaystyle\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|T_{*}\|_{2\to\infty}(\psi_{0}^{2}+|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}\|E\|\psi_{0}+|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}\psi_{1}+2^{1/2}|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}\psi_{2}),

\displaystyle\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16\sqrt{2}C_{1}(\nu)(r\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}},

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{8\sqrt{2}(\|E\|\cdot\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|EU\|_{2\to\infty})(C_{2}(\nu)\log n+2\|E\|)}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}},

\displaystyle\|Y_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{12C_{2}(\nu)\log n}{|\lambda_{r}|}+\frac{32\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|}\right)\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}.

We therefore have

\displaystyle\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{1-\tfrac{12C_{2}(\nu)\log n}{|\lambda_{r}|}-\frac{32\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|}}\bigl(\|T_{*}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\bigr),

\displaystyle\|Y_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{24C_{2}(\nu)\log n+64\delta_{r}^{-1}\|E\|^{2}}{|\lambda_{r}|}(\|T_{*}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}),

provided that
|\lambda_{r}|\geq 24C_{2}(\nu)\log n+64\delta_{r}^{-1}\|E\|^{2}
.

Wrapping up

Collecting the above bounds yields an expansion of the form

\displaystyle\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}-U|\Lambda|^{1/2}W^{(n)}=EU|\Lambda|^{-1/2}JW^{(n)}+R_{0}+R_{1}+R_{2}+Y_{0}+Y_{1}+Y_{2}

(A.44)

where
T_{*}\coloneqq EU|\Lambda|^{-1/2}JW^{(n)}
and the residual matrices
R_{0}
through
Y_{2}
satisfy

\displaystyle\|R_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{0}(\psi_{0}^{2}+|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}\|E\|\psi_{0}+|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}\psi_{1}),

\displaystyle\|R_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{0}|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}\psi_{2},

\displaystyle\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{1/2}\epsilon_{0}(\psi_{1}+\|E\|\psi_{0})}{|\lambda_{r}|}+\frac{\sqrt{2}\epsilon_{1}\psi_{0}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}\left(\exp(-18\sqrt{n})\frac{4\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{k=1}^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\psi_{3}^{(k)}|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{-(k+1/2)},

\displaystyle\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|T_{*}\|_{2\to\infty}(\psi_{0}^{2}+|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}\|E\|\psi_{0}+|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}\psi_{1}+2^{1/2}|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}\psi_{2}),

\displaystyle\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16\sqrt{2}C_{1}(\nu)(r\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{8\sqrt{2}(\|E\|\cdot\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|EU\|_{2\to\infty})(C_{2}(\nu)\log n+2\|E\|)}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}},

\displaystyle\|Y_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{24C_{2}(\nu)\log n+64\delta_{r}^{-1}\|E\|^{2}}{|\lambda_{r}|}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\bigl(\|T_{*}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\bigr).

We now bound the quantities appearing in the above expressions.
First recall that

\epsilon_{0}=\|U|\Lambda|^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq n^{1/2}\rho_{n}|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2},\quad\epsilon_{1}=\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq n^{1/2}\rho_{n}.

(A.45)

Next, recall that, according to the conditions in
Eq.

3.16
, we have

|\lambda_{r}|\geq\max\left\{24C_{2}(\nu)\log n+\frac{64\varsigma(\nu,n)^{2}}{\delta_{r}},\sqrt{n\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)}\right\},

(A.46)

where
\vartheta(c,r,n)=c\log n+r\log 9
for any
c>0
. Bounds for
\|E\|
,
\psi_{0}
, and
\psi_{1}
are almost identical to those in the positive semidefinite case, namely with probability at least
1-3n^{-\nu}
we have that

\displaystyle\|E\|\leq\varsigma(\nu,n),

(A.47)

\displaystyle\psi_{0}\leq\frac{2\varsigma(\nu,n)}{\delta_{r}},

(A.48)

\displaystyle\psi_{1}\leq 4\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}+\frac{8}{3}n\rho_{n}^{2}\lambda_{r}^{-2}\vartheta(\nu,r,n),

(A.49)

hold simultaneously. Next, by
Lemmas

6
and
A.47
, we have

\begin{split}\psi_{2}&\leq 8|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}+\frac{16n\rho_{n}^{2}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}{3|\lambda_{r}|^{5/2}}+\frac{4\varsigma(\nu,n)^{2}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}\delta_{r}},\end{split}

(A.50)

with probability at least
1-3n^{-\nu}
. Note that for bounding
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
we used the fact that
\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|U|\Lambda|^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}\leq n^{1/2}\rho_{n}|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}
.
Now by
Eq.

A.46
, we have

\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}\geq n\rho_{n}^{2}|\lambda_{r}|^{-2}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)

and the upper bounds for
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
simplify to

\displaystyle\psi_{1}\leq\frac{20}{3}\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)},

(A.51)

\displaystyle\psi_{2}\leq\frac{40}{3}|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}+\frac{4\varsigma(\nu,n)^{2}}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}.

(A.52)

Next, by
Lemma

7
, we have,
simultaneously
for all
1\leq k\leq n
that

\begin{split}\psi_{3}^{(k)}&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{k}\|_{2\to\infty}\left(\sqrt{8\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)}+\frac{8}{3}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)\right),\\
&\leq n^{1/2}\rho_{n}|\lambda_{r+1}|^{k-1}\left(\sqrt{8\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)}+\frac{8}{3}n^{1/2}\rho_{n}\lambda_{r}^{-1}\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)\right),\\
&\leq n^{1/2}\rho_{n}|\lambda_{r+1}|^{k-1}\times\frac{11}{2}\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)}\end{split}

(A.53)

with probability at least
1-n^{-\nu}
, where the final inequality follows from
Eq.

A.46
. We therefore have

\begin{split}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{\psi_{3}^{(k)}}{|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{k+1/2}}&\leq\frac{11n^{1/2}\rho_{n}^{3/2}\sqrt{\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)}}{2|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{3/2}}\sum_{k=1}^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\left|\frac{\widehat{\lambda}_{r+1}}{\lambda_{r}}\right|^{k-1}\\
&\leq\frac{11\sqrt{2}n^{1/2}\rho_{n}^{3/2}\sqrt{\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{3/2}}\sum_{k=1}^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\left(1-\frac{|\lambda_{r}|-|\lambda_{r+1}|}{|\lambda_{r}|}\right)^{k-1}\\
&\leq\frac{11\sqrt{2}n^{1/2}\rho_{n}^{3/2}\sqrt{\vartheta(\nu+2,r,n)}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}\delta_{r}}.\end{split}

(A.54)

We now bound
\|EU|\Lambda|^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}
and
\|EU\Lambda^{-1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}
. By
Lemma

8
, we have

\begin{split}\|EU\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\sqrt{8\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu+1,r,n)}+\tfrac{8}{3}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\vartheta(\nu+1,r,n)\\
&\leq\frac{11}{2}\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu+1,r,n)}\end{split}

(A.55)

with probability at least
1-n^{-\nu}
, where the final inequality follows from the fact that
\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\leq n^{1/2}\rho_{n}|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}
and
|\lambda_{r}|\geq\sqrt{n\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu+1,r,n)}
.
Eq.

A.55
also implies

\|EU|\Lambda|^{-1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\frac{11}{2}|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu+1,r,n)},

(A.56)

which yields the bound in
Eq.

3.18
. Finally, similar to the positive semidefinite case, we can show that the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
holds with probability at least
1-2n^{-\nu}
, where we take
C_{1}(\nu)=\sqrt{2(\nu+2)}
and
C_{2}(\nu)=\tfrac{2}{3}(\nu+2)
.

Now, assume the above bounds hold. Substituting the corresponding quantities into the expressions for
R_{0}
through
Y_{1}
yields

\displaystyle\|R_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}\times\frac{n\rho_{n}}{\delta_{r}^{2}},

\displaystyle\|R_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{0}\rho_{n}^{1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{|\lambda_{r}|}+\frac{\epsilon_{0}n\rho_{n}}{|\lambda_{r}|\delta_{r}},

\displaystyle\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}\left(\frac{\rho_{n}^{1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{|\lambda_{r}|}+\frac{n\rho_{n}}{|\lambda_{r}|\delta_{r}}\right)+\frac{\epsilon_{1}n\rho_{n}}{\delta_{r}^{2}|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}+\frac{n^{1/2}\rho_{n}^{3/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}\delta_{r}},

\displaystyle\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}\lesssim\frac{\rho_{n}^{1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}\left(\frac{n\rho_{n}}{\delta_{r}^{2}}+\frac{\rho_{n}^{1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{|\lambda_{r}|}\right),

\displaystyle\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\lesssim\frac{(r\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}\cdot(n\rho_{n})^{1/2}}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}+\frac{(\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\rho_{n}^{1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n}))((n\rho_{n})^{1/2}+\log n)}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}.

By
Eq.

A.45
, the bound for
\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}
can be further simplified to

\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}\lesssim\frac{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{2}}{\delta_{r}^{2}|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}+\frac{n^{1/2}\rho_{n}^{3/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}.

Similarly, the bound for
\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}
can be further simplified to

\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\lesssim\frac{(r\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}\cdot(n\rho_{n})^{1/2}}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}+\frac{\rho_{n}^{1/2}\log^{3/2}{n}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}\delta_{r}}.

We then have

\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\lesssim\frac{(r\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}\cdot(n\rho_{n})^{1/2}}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}+\frac{\rho_{n}^{1/2}\log^{3/2}{n}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}\delta_{r}},

where we had used the fact that
(n\rho_{n})/\delta_{r}^{2}\leq(n\rho_{n})^{1/2}/\delta_{r}
, recalling that
\delta_{r}\geq 4\varsigma(\nu,n)\geq(n\rho_{n})^{1/2}
(the dropped constant is immaterial here).
Combining the above bounds we obtain

\displaystyle\|R_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|R_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}\left(\frac{n\rho_{n}}{\delta_{r}^{2}}+\frac{\rho_{n}^{1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{|\lambda_{r}|}\right),

\displaystyle\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\lesssim\frac{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{2}}{\delta_{r}^{2}|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}+\frac{(\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}((rn\rho_{n})^{1/2}+\log n)}{|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}\delta_{r}}.

Recall that
T_{*}=EU|\Lambda|^{-1/2}JW^{(n)}
. Then, by
Eq.

A.56
, we have

\begin{split}\|Y_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\frac{24C_{2}(\nu)\log n+64\delta_{r}^{-1}\|E\|^{2}}{|\lambda_{r}|}\bigl(\|T_{*}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\bigr)\\
&\lesssim\frac{\rho_{n}^{1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}\Bigl(\frac{\log n}{|\lambda_{r}|}+\frac{n\rho_{n}}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|}\Bigr)+\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\\
&\lesssim\frac{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{2}}{\delta_{r}^{2}|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}+\frac{(\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}((rn\rho_{n})^{1/2}+\log n)}{|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}\delta_{r}},\end{split}

where the second inequality follows from the fact that
|\lambda_{r}|\geq 24C_{2}(\nu)\log n+64\tfrac{\varsigma(\nu,n)^{2}}{\delta_{r}}
, and the third inequality follows from the fact that
(n\rho_{n})/(|\lambda_{r}|\delta_{r})\leq(n\rho_{n})/\delta_{r}^{2}\leq(n\rho_{n})^{1/2}/\delta_{r}
.
In summary, letting
\widetilde{R}=R_{0}+R_{1}+R_{2}+Y_{0}+Y_{1}+Y_{2}
, we have

\begin{split}\|\widetilde{R}\|_{2\to\infty}&\lesssim\epsilon_{0}\left(\frac{n\rho_{n}}{\delta_{r}^{2}}+\frac{\rho_{n}^{1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{|\lambda_{r}|}\right)+\frac{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{2}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}\delta_{r}^{2}}+\frac{(\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}((rn\rho_{n})^{1/2}+\log n)}{|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}\delta_{r}}.\end{split}

Finally, as
\epsilon_{0}\leq n^{1/2}\rho_{n}|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}
, the above bound simplifies to

\begin{split}\|\widetilde{R}\|_{2\to\infty}&\lesssim\frac{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{2}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}\delta_{r}^{2}}+\frac{(\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}\left(\frac{(rn\rho_{n})^{1/2}+\log n}{\delta_{r}}\right),\end{split}

as desired. This concludes the proof of
Theorem

2
.

A.4
Proof of
Theorem

3

In this proof, for conciseness, we briefly sketch the main steps of the argument, since the technical details are similar to the proofs for
Theorems

1
and
2
. Note that we will, on numerous occasions, silently apply
Eq.

A.2
to replace
\widehat{\lambda}_{r}
with
\lambda_{r}
.

First, write
\widehat{U}-UW^{(n)}
as

\widehat{U}-UW^{(n)}=\widehat{U}-UU^{\top}\widehat{U}+UU^{\top}\widehat{U}-UW^{(n)}.

For
R_{0}\coloneqq UU^{\top}\widehat{U}-UW^{(n)}
, we have

\|R_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\times\psi_{0}^{2}.

Next, writing
\widehat{U}=A\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1}=P\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}+P\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}
, we have

\widehat{U}-UU^{\top}\widehat{U}=(I-UU^{\top})P\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}+E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}-UU^{\top}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}.

Consequently,

\|UU^{\top}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\frac{2\|U\|_{2\to\infty}(\psi_{1}+\|E\|\psi_{0})}{|\lambda_{r}|}.

The term
(I-UU^{\top})P\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}
admits the expansion

\begin{split}\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}&=\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{m}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-m}+\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{k}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}\end{split}

for any
m\geq 2
. Setting
m=n\rho_{n}^{1/2}+1
, the same argument as for
Eqs.

A.41
and
A.8
yields

\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{m}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-m}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\begin{cases}2\epsilon_{1}\psi_{0}|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}\exp(-9n^{1/2}),&\text{if $P$ is indefinite}\\
\sqrt{2}\rho_{n}^{1/2}\psi_{0}\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}\exp(-9n^{1/2})&\text{if $P$ is definite}\end{cases}.

(A.57)

Next, if
\kappa
is positive semidefinite, then using the same derivations as for
Theorem

1
gives

\Bigl\|\sum_{k=1}^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{k}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}\Bigr\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\frac{2^{5/2}\rho_{n}^{1/2}\|E\|\psi_{0}}{\lambda_{r}^{1/2}\delta_{r}}+\sum_{k=1}^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\psi_{3}^{(k)}\widehat{\lambda}_{k}^{-(k+1)}.

Otherwise, if
\kappa
is indefinite, then using the same derivations as for
Theorem

2
gives

\Bigl\|\sum_{k=1}^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{k}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}\Bigr\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\frac{8\epsilon_{1}\|E\|\psi_{0}}{|\lambda_{r}|\delta_{r}}+\sum_{k=1}^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\psi_{3}^{(k)}\times|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{-(k+1)}

Next, for
E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}
, we have

E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}=EU\Lambda^{-1}[W^{(n}+(U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)})+(\Lambda U^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}-U^{\top}\widehat{U})]+E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}.

Here,

\displaystyle\|EU\Lambda^{-1}(U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)})\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|EU\Lambda^{-1}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\psi_{0}^{2},

\displaystyle\|EU\Lambda^{-1}(\Lambda U^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}-U^{\top}\widehat{U})\|_{2\to\infty}\leq 2|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}(\|EU\Lambda^{-1}\|_{2\to\infty}(\psi_{1}+\|E\|\psi_{0}).

Finally,
Lemma

2
implies

\displaystyle\|E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}\|_{2\to\infty}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{32C_{1}(\nu)(r\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{16(\|E\|\cdot\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|EU\|_{2\to\infty})(C_{2}(\nu)\log n+2\|E\|)}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\Bigl(\frac{12C_{2}(\nu)\log n}{|\lambda_{r}|}+\frac{32\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|}\Bigr).

Now suppose
\kappa
is positive semidefinite and
\lambda_{r}\geq 24C_{2}(\nu)\log n+64\delta_{r}^{-1}\|E\|^{2}
. Collecting the above bounds we obtain an expression of the form

\widehat{U}-UW^{(n)}=EU\Lambda^{-1}W^{(n)}+R_{0}+R_{1}+R_{2}+Y_{0}+Y_{1},

where, with
T_{*}=EU\Lambda^{-1}W^{(n)}
, we have

\displaystyle\|R_{0}\|\leq\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\times\psi_{0}^{2},\quad\|R_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq 2\lambda_{r}^{-1}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}(\psi_{1}+\|E\|\psi_{0}),

\displaystyle\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}\rho_{n}^{1/2}\psi_{0}}{\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}\Bigl(\exp(-9\sqrt{n})+\frac{4\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}\Bigr)+\sum_{k=1}^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\psi_{3}^{(k)}\widehat{\lambda}_{r}^{-(k+1)}

\displaystyle\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|T_{*}\|_{2\to\infty}(2\lambda_{r}^{-1}(\psi_{1}+\|E\|\psi_{0})+\psi_{0}^{2})+\frac{32C_{1}(\nu)(r\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}\lambda_{r}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{16(\|E\|\cdot\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|EU\|_{2\to\infty})(C_{\nu}\log n+2\|E\|)}{\delta_{r}\lambda_{r}},

\displaystyle\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\frac{24C_{2}(\nu)\log n+64\delta_{r}^{-1}\|E\|^{2}}{\lambda_{r}}\bigl(\|T_{*}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|R_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}\bigr).

Once again, substituting the bounds for
\psi_{0},\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\psi_{3}^{(k)}
and
\|E\|
in the proof of
Theorem

1
together with some involved calculations, we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{U}W^{(n)\top}-U=EU\Lambda^{-1}+\widetilde{R},

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{R}\|_{2\to\infty}\lesssim\frac{n\rho_{n}^{3/2}}{\lambda_{r}^{1/2}\delta_{r}^{2}}+\frac{(\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}}{\lambda_{r}}\Bigl(\frac{\log n}{\delta_{r}}+\frac{(rn\rho_{n})^{1/2}}{\delta_{r}}\Bigr)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-\nu})
. The derivations when
\kappa
is indefinite is similar and thus omitted.

For
\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}-U\Lambda W^{(n)}
, we have

\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}-U\Lambda W^{(n)}=(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}+U(U^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}-\Lambda U^{\top}\widehat{U})+U\Lambda(U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)}).

For
R_{0}\coloneqq U\Lambda(U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)})
, we have

\|R_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|U\Lambda\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)}\|\leq n^{1/2}\rho_{n}\times\psi_{0}^{2}.

Next, for
R_{1}\coloneqq U(U^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}-\Lambda U^{\top}\widehat{U})
we have

\begin{split}\|R_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|U^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}-\Lambda U^{\top}\widehat{U}\|\\
&\leq\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|U^{\top}E\widehat{U}\|\\
&\leq\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\times(\psi_{1}+\|E\|\psi_{0}).\end{split}

The term
(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}
has the expansion

\begin{split}(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}&=(I-UU^{\top})E\widehat{U}+(I-UU)^{\top}P\widehat{U}\\
&=E\widehat{U}+UU^{\top}E\widehat{U}+(I-UU^{\top})P^{m+1}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-m}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}(I-UU^{\top})P^{k}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-k}\end{split}

for any
m\geq 1
.
Once again, we have

\|UU^{\top}E\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\times(\psi_{1}+\mathbb{E}\|\psi_{0}).

Setting
m=n\rho_{n}^{1/2}+1
we have,

\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{m+1}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-m}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\begin{cases}\epsilon_{1}\psi_{0}\exp(-9n^{1/2}),&\text{if $P$ is indefinite}\\
\rho_{n}^{1/2}\psi_{0}\lambda_{r+1}^{1/2}\exp(-9n^{1/2})&\text{if $P$ is definite}\end{cases}.

(A.58)

Next, if
\kappa
is positive semidefinite, then using the same derivations as for
Theorem

1
, we have

\Bigl\|\sum_{k=1}^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{k}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-k}\Bigr\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\frac{2^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{1/2}\lambda_{r}^{1/2}\|E\|\psi_{0}}{\delta_{r}}+\sum_{k=1}^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\psi_{3}^{(k)}\widehat{\lambda}_{r}^{-k}.

Otherwise, if
\kappa
is indefinite, then using the same derivations as for
Theorem

2
, we have

\Bigl\|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Pi_{U}^{\perp}P^{k}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-k}\Bigr\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\frac{4\epsilon_{1}\|E\|\psi_{0}}{\delta_{r}}+\sum_{k=1}^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\psi_{3}^{(k)}|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{-k}.

Next, write

E\widehat{U}=EU[W^{(n}+(U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)})]+E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}.

Lemma

2
then implies

\begin{split}\|E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\frac{16C_{1}(\nu)(r\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{8(\|E\|\cdot\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|EU\|_{2\to\infty})(C_{\nu}\log n+2\|E\|)}{\delta_{r}}\\
&\qquad+\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}\|_{2\to\infty}\Bigl(\frac{12C_{2}(\nu)\log n}{|\lambda_{r}|}+\frac{32\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|}\Bigr).\end{split}

Now, suppose
\kappa
is positive definite and
\lambda_{r}\geq 24C_{2}(\nu)\log n+64\delta_{r}^{-1}\|E\|^{2}
. Collecting the above bounds we obtain an expression of the form

\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}-U\Lambda W^{(n)}=EUW^{(n)}+R_{0}+R_{1}+R_{2}+Y_{0}+Y_{1},

where, with
T_{*}=EUW^{(n)}
, we have

\displaystyle\|R_{0}\|\leq n^{1/2}\rho_{n}\times\psi_{0}^{2},\quad\|R_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|U\|_{2\to\infty}(\psi_{1}+\|E\|\psi_{0}),

\displaystyle\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}\psi_{0}\Bigl(\lambda_{r+1}^{1/2}\exp(-9n^{1/2})+\frac{2^{3/2}\lambda_{r}^{1/2}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}\Bigr)+\sum_{m=1}^{n\rho_{n}^{1/2}}\psi_{3}^{(k)}\widehat{\lambda}_{r}^{-k}

\displaystyle\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|T_{*}\|_{2\to\infty}\psi_{0}^{2}+\frac{16C_{1}(\nu)(r\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{8(\|E\|\cdot\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|EU\|_{2\to\infty})(C_{\nu}\log n+2\|E\|)}{\delta_{r}},

\displaystyle\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\frac{24C_{2}(\nu)\log n+64\delta_{r}^{-1}\|E\|^{2}}{|\lambda_{r}|}\bigl(\|T_{*}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|R_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}+\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}\bigr).

Once again, substituting the bounds for
\psi_{0},\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\psi_{3}^{(k)}
and
\|E\|
in the proof of
Theorem

1
together with some involved calculations, we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}W^{(n)\top}-U\Lambda=EU+\widetilde{R},

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{R}\|_{2\to\infty}\lesssim\frac{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{2}}{\delta_{r}^{2}}+(\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}\Bigl(\frac{\log n}{\delta_{r}}+\frac{(rn\rho_{n})^{1/2}}{\delta_{r}}\Bigr)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-\nu})
. The derivations when
\kappa
is similar and we therefore omit the details. This completes the proof of
Theorem

3
.

A.5
Deterministic perturbation bound

We now state a deterministic perturbation
bound for the
r
leading eigenvectors
U
of a symmetric matrix
M

when it is additively perturbed by an arbitrary noise matrix
E
. The
result can be viewed as a generalization or reformulation of

Eq.

3.30
in
Theorem

3
. While there
are numerous terms in the expansion, they all depend on quantities
that are
linear
in
E
and thus, for many inference problems,
can be bounded using standard matrix perturbation and matrix
concentration inequalities. In particular
Eq.

3.30

follows from
Theorem

5
by bounding
\|E\|
using

Lemma

4
, bounding
\psi_{0}
using the Davis-Kahan
theorem (given an upper bound for
\|E\|
), bounding
\psi_{1}
using

Lemma

5
, bounding
\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU\|_{2\to\infty}
using

Lemma

7
, bounding
\|EU\|_{2\to\infty}
and

\psi_{*}
using
Lemma

8
, bounding
\eta
using

Lemma

9
, and finally, verifying

Eq.

A.60
using a matrix Bernstein inequality (see

Eq.

A.19
).

Theorem 5

(Deterministic row-wise eigenvector perturbation bound)
.

Let
M
and
E
be symmetric
n\times n
matrices, and define
\widehat{M}=M+E
. For a given
r\geq 1
, let the diagonal matrices
\widehat{\Lambda}
and
\Lambda
contain the
r
largest in magnitude eigenvalues of
\widehat{M}
and
M
, respectively, and let
\widehat{U}
and
U
be the
n\times r
matrices whose orthonormal columns are the corresponding eigenvectors of
\widehat{M}
and
M
. Also, for
h\in[n]
, denote by
\widehat{M}^{[h]}
the matrix obtained by replacing the entries in the
h
th row and
h
th column of
\widehat{M}
with those in the
h
th row and
h
th column of
M
, and let
\widehat{U}^{[h]}
be defined similar to
\widehat{U}
, but with
\widehat{M}^{[h]}
in place of
\widehat{M}
. Let
V^{[h]}=(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}^{[h]}
and
\delta_{r}=|\lambda_{r}|-|\lambda_{r+1}|
. Suppose there exists quantities
\alpha>0
and
\beta>0
such that the following conditions

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{0}=\{\delta_{r}\geq\max\{4\|E\|,8\beta\}\},

(A.59)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}=\{\forall h\in[n],\|e_{h}^{\top}EV^{[h]}\|\leq\alpha\|V^{[h]}\|_{F}+\beta\|V^{[h]}\|_{2\to\infty}\},

(A.60)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}=\{|\lambda_{r}|\geq 24\beta+64\delta_{r}^{-1}\|E\|\}.

(A.61)

hold simultaneously.
We then have

\widehat{U}W^{(n)}-U=EU\Lambda^{-1}+R

where the residual matrix
R
satisfies

\displaystyle\|R\|_{2\to\infty}\leq r_{2,\infty}^{(0)}+r_{2,\infty}^{(1)}+r_{2,\infty}^{(2)}+y_{2,\infty}^{(0)}+y_{2,\infty}^{(1)},

\displaystyle r_{2,\infty}^{(0)}=\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\psi_{0}^{2},\quad r_{2,\infty}^{(1)}=\frac{2\|U\|_{2\to\infty}(\psi_{1}+\|E\|\psi_{0})}{|\lambda_{r}|},

\displaystyle r_{2,\infty}^{(2)}=\frac{8\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|E\|\psi_{0}}{|\lambda_{r}|\delta_{r}}+\frac{4\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU\|_{2\to\infty}}{\lambda_{r}^{2}}+\frac{16\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\eta}{\lambda_{r}^{2}\delta_{r}}

\displaystyle y_{2,\infty}^{(0)}=\psi_{*}\Bigl(\frac{\psi_{1}+\|E\|\psi_{0}}{2|\lambda_{r}|}+\psi_{0}^{2}\Bigr)+\frac{32\alpha r^{1/2}\|E\|}{|\lambda_{r}|\delta_{r}}+\frac{(\|E\|\cdot\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|EU\|_{2\to\infty})(16\beta+32\|E\|)}{\delta_{r}|\lambda_{r}|},

\displaystyle y_{2,\infty}^{(1)}=\frac{24\beta+64\delta_{r}^{-1}\|E\|^{2}}{|\lambda_{r}|}\Bigl(\psi_{*}+r_{2,\infty}^{(1)}+r_{2,\infty}^{(2)}+y_{2,\infty}^{(0)}\Bigr)

and
\psi_{0},\psi_{1},\eta,\psi_{*}
are defined as

\displaystyle\psi_{0}:=\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|,\quad\psi_{1}:=\|U^{\top}EU\|,\quad\eta:=\|\Lambda_{\perp}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU\|,\quad\psi_{*}:=\|EU\Lambda^{-1}\|_{2\to\infty}.

If
M
is positive semidefinite then the quantity
r_{2,\infty}^{(2)}
can be simplified to

\displaystyle r_{2,\infty}^{(2)}=\frac{2^{5/2}\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|E\|\psi_{0}}{\lambda_{r}^{1/2}\delta_{r}}+\frac{8\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\widetilde{\eta}}{\lambda_{r}\delta_{r}}

where now
\widetilde{\eta}:=\|\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU\|
.

Proof.

The terms
r_{2,\infty}^{(0)},r_{2,\infty}^{(1)}
are derived the same way as the upper bounds for
\|R_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}
and
\|R_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}

in the proof of
Theorem

3
.
Next, if
\mathcal{E}_{1}
holds, then we can apply the same arguments as that in the proof of
Lemma

2
to obtain

\begin{split}\|E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|&\leq\frac{16\alpha r^{1/2}\|E\|}{\delta_{r}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{(\|E\|\cdot\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\|EU\|_{2\to\infty})(8\beta+16\|E\|)}{\delta_{r}}\\
&\qquad+\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|_{2\to\infty}\left(6\beta+\frac{16\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}}\right).\end{split}

(A.62)

Given
Eq.

A.62
, the terms
y_{2,\infty}^{(0)}
and
y_{2,\infty}^{(1)}
are derived the same way as the upper bounds for
\|Y_{0}\|_{2\to\infty}
and
\|Y_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}
in the proof of
Theorem

3
. We now derive the expression for
r_{2,\infty}^{(2)}
, which is similar to the upper bound for
\|R_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}
in
Theorem

3
.

First, we have

(I-UU^{\top})M\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}=(I-UU^{\top})M^{m}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-m}+\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}(I-UU^{\top})M^{k}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}

which holds for any
m\geq 2
.
We also have

\Pi_{U}^{\perp}M^{k}E\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}=\Pi_{U}^{\perp}M^{k}E\Pi_{U}^{\perp}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}+\Pi_{U}^{\perp}M^{k}EUU^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}

where
\Pi_{U}^{\perp}=I-UU^{\top}
.
Now suppose
M
is positive semidefinite. Then

\begin{split}\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}M^{k}E\Pi_{U}^{\perp}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|\Lambda_{\perp}\|^{k-1/2}\times\|E\|\times\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|\times\|\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}\|\\
&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|E\|\times\psi_{0}\times|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{-3/2}\times\Bigl(\frac{\lambda_{r+1}}{|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|}\Bigr)^{k-1/2}\\
&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|E\|\times\psi_{0}\times 2^{3/2}\lambda_{r}^{-3/2}\times\Bigl(1-\frac{\lambda_{r}-\lambda_{r+1}}{2\lambda_{r}}\Bigr)^{k-1/2}\end{split}

where the final inequality follows from the same argument as that in
Eqs.

A.6
and
A.2
.
Similarly, we also have

\begin{split}\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}M^{k}EUU^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|\Lambda_{\perp}\|^{k-1}\times\|\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU\|\times\|U^{\top}\widehat{U}\|\times\|\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}\|\\
&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\widetilde{\eta}\times|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{-2}\times\Bigl(\frac{\lambda_{r+1}}{|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|}\Bigr)^{k-1}\\
&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\widetilde{\eta}\times 4\lambda_{r}^{-2}\times\Bigl(1-\frac{\lambda_{r}-\lambda_{r+1}}{2\lambda_{r}}\Bigr)^{k-1/2}.\end{split}

Finally,
