\|(I-UU^{\top})M^{m}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-m}\|\leq\Bigl(1-\frac{\lambda_{r}-\lambda_{r+1}}{2\lambda_{r}}\Bigr)^{m}.

As
\delta_{r}=\lambda_{r}-\lambda_{r+1}>0
, we have

\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\|(I-UU^{\top})M^{m}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-m}\|\leq\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\Bigl(1-\frac{\lambda_{r}-\lambda_{r+1}}{2\lambda_{r}}\Bigr)^{m}=0.

We also have

\begin{split}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}M^{k}E\Pi_{U}^{\perp}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq 2^{3/2}\lambda_{r}^{-3/2}\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|E\|\psi_{0}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Bigl(1-\frac{\lambda_{r}-\lambda_{r+1}}{2\lambda_{r}}\Bigr)^{k-1/2}\\
&\leq 2^{5/2}\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}\delta_{r}^{-1}\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|E\|\psi_{0}\end{split}

\begin{split}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}M^{k}EUU^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq 4\lambda_{r}^{-2}\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\widetilde{\eta}\times\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Bigl(1-\frac{\lambda_{r}-\lambda_{r+1}}{2\lambda_{r}}\Bigr)^{k-1}\\
&\leq 8\lambda_{r}^{-1}\delta_{r}^{-1}\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\widetilde{\eta}\end{split}

Combining the above inequalities we obtain the expression

r_{2,\infty}^{(2)}=\frac{2^{5/2}\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|E\|\psi_{0}}{\lambda_{r}^{1/2}\delta_{r}}+\frac{8\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\widetilde{\eta}}{\lambda_{r}\delta_{r}}

when
M
is positive semidefinite.

Now, suppose
M
is possibly indefinite. We then have

\begin{split}(I-UU^{\top})M\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}&=(I-UU^{\top})M^{m}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-m}+(I-UU^{\top})MEUU^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\\
&+\sum_{k=2}^{m-1}(I-UU^{\top})M^{k}UU^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}+\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}(I-UU^{\top})M^{k}(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}\end{split}

for any
m\geq 3
. For each
k\geq 1
we have

\begin{split}\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}M^{k}E\Pi_{U}^{\perp}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|\Lambda_{\perp}\|^{k-1}\times\|E\|\times\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|\times\|\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}\|\\
&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}\times|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{-2}\times\|E\|\times\psi_{0}\times\Bigl(\frac{|\lambda_{r+1}|}{|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|}\Bigr)^{k-1}\\
&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}\times 4|\lambda_{r}|^{-2}\times\|E\|\psi_{0}\times\Bigl(1-\frac{|\lambda_{r}|-|\lambda_{r+1}|}{2|\lambda_{r}|}\Bigr)^{k-1}.\end{split}

Similarly, for each
k\geq 2
we have

\begin{split}\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}M^{k}EUU^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|\Lambda_{\perp}\|^{k-2}\times\|\Lambda_{\perp}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU\|\times\|U^{\top}\widehat{U}\|\times\|\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}\|\\
&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\eta\times|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|^{-3}\times\Bigl(\frac{|\lambda_{r+1}|}{|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}|}\Bigr)^{k-2}\\
&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\eta\times 8|\lambda_{r}|^{-3}\times\Bigl(1-\frac{|\lambda_{r}|-|\lambda_{r+1}|}{2|\lambda_{r}|}\Bigr)^{k-2}\end{split}

Note that we avoided bounding
\|U_{\perp}|\Lambda_{\perp}|^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}
directly when
M
is indefinite.
Then by taking
m\rightarrow\infty
obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\|(I-UU^{\top})M^{m}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-m}\|=0,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}M^{k}E\Pi_{U}^{\perp}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq 8|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}\delta_{r}^{-1}\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|E\|\psi_{0},

\displaystyle\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\|\Pi_{U}^{\perp}M^{k}EUU^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-(k+1)}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq 16\lambda_{r}^{-2}\delta_{r}^{-1}\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\eta

Finally, we have

\begin{split}\|(I-UU^{\top})MEUU^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\|(I-UU^{\top})MEU\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|U^{\top}\widehat{U}\|\times|\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\|\\
&\leq\|(I-UU^{\top})MEU\|_{2\to\infty}\times 4\lambda_{r}^{-2}\end{split}

Combining the above bounds we obtain the expression

r_{2,\infty}^{(2)}=\frac{4\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU\|_{2\to\infty}}{\lambda_{r}^{2}}+\frac{8\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|E\|\psi_{0}}{|\lambda_{r}|\delta_{r}}+\frac{16\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\eta}{\lambda_{r}^{2}\delta_{r}}

when
M
is indefinite. This concludes the proof of
Theorem

5
.
∎

A.6
Proof of
Corollary

3

Write
Z_{r}=U|\Lambda|^{1/2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}
and
\widehat{Z}_{r}=\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}W^{(n)}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}
.
Define
\widehat{P}_{r}=\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}\widehat{U}^{\top}\equiv\widehat{Z}_{r}J\widehat{Z}_{r}^{\top}
and
P_{r}=U\Lambda U^{\top}\equiv Z_{r}JZ_{r}^{\top}
, where
J
is the diagonal matrix with diagonal elements
+1
and
-1
such that
\Lambda=|\Lambda|J
.
Further define
\xi_{r}=\|\widehat{Z}_{r}-Z_{r}\|_{2\to\infty}
. As

\|M_{1}M_{2}^{\top}\|_{\max}\leq\|M_{1}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|M_{2}\|_{2\to\infty}
for conformable matrices
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
, we have

\begin{split}\|\widehat{P}_{r}-P_{r}\|_{\max}&=\|Z_{r}J(Z_{r}-\widehat{Z}_{r})^{\top}+(Z_{r}-\widehat{Z}_{r})JZ_{r}^{\top}-(Z_{r}-\widehat{Z}_{r})J(Z_{r}-\widehat{Z}_{r})^{\top}\|_{\max}\\
&\leq 2\|Z_{r}J(Z_{r}-\widehat{Z}_{r})^{\top}\|_{\max}+\|(Z_{r}-\widehat{Z}_{r})J(Z_{r}-\widehat{Z}_{r})^{\top}\|_{\max}\\
&=2\|Z_{r}J(EU\Lambda^{-1/2}+Q)^{\top}\|_{\max}+\xi_{r}^{2}\\
&\leq 2\|UJU^{\top}E\|_{\max}+2\|Z_{r}\|_{2\to\infty}\cdot\|Q\|_{2\to\infty}+\xi_{r}^{2}\\
&\leq 2\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\cdot\|EU\|_{2\to\infty}+2\|U|\Lambda|^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\cdot\|Q\|_{2\to\infty}+\xi_{r}^{2}.\end{split}

(A.63)

Now, supposing
\kappa
is a positive semidefinite kernel, we have

\begin{split}\rho_{n}^{-1}\|\widehat{P}_{r}-P_{r}\|_{\max}&\leq 2\rho_{n}^{-1}\|EU\|_{2\to\infty}\cdot\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\rho_{n}^{-1/2}\|Q\|_{2\to\infty}+(\rho_{n}^{-1/2}\xi_{r})^{2},\end{split}

(A.64)

since
\|U\Lambda^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}
. In this setting,
Lemma

8
gives

\|EU\|_{2\to\infty}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})

(A.65)

with high probability. Further, supposing
r
is chosen so that the conditions in
Eq.

3.8
are satisfied (which can be achieved simply by using the eigenvalues
\{\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\}_{j\geq 1}
of
A
, similar to
Corollary

1
), then

\|Q\|_{2\to\infty}=o(\rho_{n}^{1/2}\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n}))\quad\text{and}\quad\xi_{r}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{1/2}\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})

(A.66)

with high probability. As
\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}
, combining
Eqs.

A.65
and
A.66
yields

\begin{split}\rho_{n}^{-1}\|\widehat{P}_{r}-P_{r}\|_{\max}\lesssim\frac{(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{\lambda_{r}^{1/2}}\end{split}

(A.67)

with high probability. Hence, under the aforementioned assumptions, we arrive at the entrywise bound

\begin{split}\rho_{n}^{-1}\|\widehat{P}_{r}-P\|_{\max}&\leq\rho_{n}^{-1}\|P_{r}-P\|_{\max}+O\left(\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}n)\right),\end{split}

(A.68)

which holds with high probability.

Next, suppose instead that
\kappa
is possibly indefinite. Then, in place of
Eq.

A.64
, we have

\begin{split}\rho_{n}^{-1}\|\widehat{P}_{r}-P_{r}\|_{\max}&\leq 2\rho_{n}^{-1}\|EU\|_{2\to\infty}\cdot\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\frac{n^{1/2}}{|\lambda_{r}|^{1/2}}\|Q\|_{2\to\infty}+(\rho_{n}^{-1/2}\xi_{r})^{2},\end{split}

(A.69)

since
\|U|\Lambda|^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq n^{1/2}\rho_{n}|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}
per
Remark

8
.
Eq.

A.65
still holds under the indefinite setting while
Eq.

A.66
holds with
|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}
in place of
\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}
. Finally
\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\leq n^{1/2}\rho_{n}|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}
, and hence, by combining
Eqs.

A.65
and
A.66
, we obtain the more widely-applicable entrywise bounds

\displaystyle\rho_{n}^{-1}\|\widehat{P}_{r}-P_{r}\|_{\max}\lesssim\frac{(n\rho_{n})^{1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{|\lambda_{r}|},

(A.70)

\displaystyle\rho_{n}^{-1}\|\widehat{P}_{r}-P\|_{\max}\leq\rho_{n}^{-1}\|P_{r}-P\|_{\max}+O\left(|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}(n\rho_{n})^{1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n}\right),

(A.71)

which holds with high probability. This concludes the proof of
Corollary

3
.

A.7
Proof of
Proposition

2

Let
\mathcal{H}
denote the reproducing kernel Hilbert space (RKHS) associated with
\kappa
. Given
\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, let
\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{H},n}
denote the linear operator

\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{H},n}\eta=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\eta,\Phi(X_{i})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\Phi(X_{i}),\qquad\text{for all }\eta\in\mathcal{H},

where
\Phi(x)=\kappa(\cdot,x)\in\mathcal{H}
denotes the reproducing element at
x\in\Omega
. The (non-zero) eigenvalues of
\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{H},n}
are the same as those of
\rho_{n}^{-1}P
, while the eigenfunctions of
\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{H},n}
are
extensions
of the corresponding eigenvectors of
\rho_{n}^{-1}P
. See
[
47
]
for more details.

Let
\widehat{\Pi}_{r}
denote the projection operator onto the
r
-dimensional subspaces spanned by the
r
leading eigenvectors of
\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{H},n}
, and let
p^{(r)}_{ij}
denote the
ij
-th entry of
P_{r}
. We then have

p^{(r)}_{ij}=\rho_{n}\langle\widehat{\Pi}_{r}\Phi(X_{i}),\widehat{\Pi}_{r}\Phi(X_{j})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}.

See the proof of
[
53
, Lemma 3.4]
for a derivation of the above identity. Consequently,

\begin{split}\rho_{n}^{-1}|p^{(r)}_{ij}-p_{ij}|&=\left|\langle\widehat{\Pi}_{r}\Phi(X_{i}),\widehat{\Pi}_{r}\Phi(X_{j})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}-\langle\Phi(X_{i}),\Phi(X_{j})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\right|\\
&\leq\|(\widehat{\Pi}_{r}-\Pi_{r})\Phi(X_{i})\|_{\mathcal{H}}\times\|\Pi_{r}\Phi(X_{j})\|_{\mathcal{H}}\\
&\qquad+\|(\widehat{\Pi}_{r}-\Pi_{r})\Phi(X_{j})\|_{\mathcal{H}}\times\|\Pi_{r}\Phi(X_{i})\|_{\mathcal{H}}\\
&\qquad+\|(\widehat{\Pi}_{r}-\Pi_{r})\Phi(X_{i})\|_{\mathcal{H}}\times\|(\widehat{\Pi}_{r}-\Pi_{r})\Phi(X_{j})\|_{\mathcal{H}}\\
&\qquad+\left|\langle\Pi_{r}\Phi(X_{i}),\Pi_{r}\Phi(X_{j})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}-\langle\Phi(X_{i}),\Phi(X_{j})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\right|,\end{split}

(A.72)

where
\Pi_{r}
is the projection onto the subspaces spanned by the
r
leading
eigenfunctions
of
\mathcal{K}
. We now have

\begin{split}\|(\widehat{\Pi}_{r}-\Pi_{r})\Phi(X_{i})\|_{\mathcal{H}}&\leq\|\widehat{\Pi}_{r}-\Pi_{r}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\times\|\Phi(X_{i})\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq\kappa(X_{i},X_{i})\times\|\widehat{\Pi}_{r}-\Pi_{r}\|_{\mathcal{H}}.\end{split}

(A.73)

Perturbation bounds for projection operators (e.g., Proposition 6 and Theorem 7 in
[
47
]
) yield

\|\widehat{\Pi}_{r}-\Pi_{r}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\lesssim\frac{\log^{1/2}{n}}{n^{1/2}(\mu_{r}-\mu_{r+1})}

(A.74)

with high probability. Next, by Mercer’s theorem
[
51
, Corollary 4.50]
,
\{\sqrt{\mu_{r}}\phi_{r}\}_{r\geq 1}
forms an orthonormal basis for
\mathcal{H}
, and we can write

\Pi_{r}\Phi(X_{i})=\sum_{k=1}^{r}\langle\sqrt{\mu_{k}}\phi_{k}(\cdot),\Phi(X_{i})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\mu_{k}}\phi_{k}(\cdot)=\sum_{k=1}^{r}\mu_{k}\phi_{k}(X_{i})\phi_{k}(\cdot),

and
\Phi(X_{i})=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\mu_{k}\phi_{k}(X_{i})\phi_{k}(\cdot)
. Here, the final equality in the expression for
\Pi_{r}\Phi(X_{i})
follows from the reproducing kernel property of
\Phi
. Finally, the inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}
can be manipulated using
[
51
, Theorem 4.51]
to yield

\displaystyle\langle\Pi_{r}\Phi(X_{i}),\Pi_{r}\Phi(X_{j})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\sum_{k=1}^{r}\mu_{k}\phi_{k}(X_{i})\phi_{k}(X_{j}),

(A.75)

\displaystyle\langle\Phi(X_{i}),\Phi(X_{j})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\mu_{k}\phi_{k}(X_{i})\phi_{k}(X_{j}).

(A.76)

Plugging
Eqs.

A.76
,
A.75
,
A.74
and
A.73
into
Eq.

A.72
, we obtain

\begin{split}\max_{ij}\rho_{n}^{-1}|p_{ij}^{(r)}-p_{ij}|&\lesssim\frac{\log^{1/2}{n}}{n^{1/2}(\mu_{r}-\mu_{r+1})}+\max_{ij}\left|\sum_{k>r}\mu_{k}\phi_{k}(X_{i})\phi_{k}(X_{j})\right|\end{split}

(A.77)

with high probability. This concludes the proof of Proposition
2
.

A.8
Proof of
Theorem

4

Let
\widehat{\xi}_{i}
and
\xi_{i}
denote the
i
-th row of
\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}
and
U\Lambda^{1/2}
, respectively. Suppose that the null hypothesis
\mathbb{H}_{0}\colon X_{i}=X_{j}
is true. We first note that a direct application of
Theorem

3
is sub-optimal as then, to make
\|Q\|_{2\to\infty}=o_{p}(\rho_{n}^{1/2})
, we need
\delta_{r}^{2}=\omega((n\rho_{n})^{3/2}
which leads to a more stringent condition than
\delta_{r}=\omega(\sqrt{n\rho_{n}\log n})
given in
Theorem

4
. Rather, we need to exploit the fact that we can drop the term
n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{2}\delta_{r}^{-2}
from the bound for
Q
under
\mathbb{H}_{0}
. Indeed, from the proof of
Theorem

3
, we have the decomposition

\begin{split}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}-U\Lambda W^{(n)}&=U\Lambda(U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)})+U(U^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}-\Lambda U^{\top}\widehat{U})+UU^{\top}E\widehat{U}+(I-UU)^{\top}P\widehat{U}\\
&\qquad+EU[W^{(n)}+(U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)}]+E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}.\end{split}

(A.78)

Then, under
\mathbb{H}_{0}
, the
i
-th and
j
-th row of all matrix products in
Eq.

A.78
whose first term is either
U
or
(I-UU^{\top})
are the same. Hence

\widehat{\xi}_{i}-\widehat{\xi}_{j}=(E_{i}-E_{j})U[W^{(n)}+(U^{\top}\widehat{U}-W^{(n)}]+(E_{i}-E_{j})(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U},

where
E_{i}
and
E_{j}
are the
i
-th and
j
-th row of
E
, respectively. Finally, by following the remaining steps in the proof of
Theorem

3
, we obtain

\widehat{\xi}_{i}-\widehat{\xi}_{j}=(E_{i}-E_{j})UW^{(n)}+\gamma_{ij},\quad\|\gamma_{ij}\|=O\Bigl[(\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}\Bigl(\frac{\log n}{\delta_{r}}+\frac{(rn\rho_{n})^{1/2}}{\delta_{r}}\Bigr)\Bigr]

(A.79)

with high probability.
Now, choose
r
such that the conditions

\displaystyle\delta_{r}=\omega\left(\max\left\{\log^{3/2}{n},\sqrt{rn\rho_{n}\log n}\right\}\right)

(A.80)

is satisfied. Then,
Eq.

A.79
implies

\widehat{\xi}_{i}-\widehat{\xi}_{j}=(E_{i}-E_{j})U+\gamma_{ij},\quad\|\gamma_{ij}\|=o(\rho_{n}^{1/2})

with high probability.
We therefore have

\Bigl|\|\widehat{\xi}_{i}-\widehat{\xi}_{j}\|^{2}-\|(E_{i}-E_{j})U\|^{2}\Bigr|=o(\rho_{n})+o(\rho_{n}^{1/2})\times\|(E_{i}-E_{j})U\|

with high probability.

The remainder of this proof is devoted to analyzing
\widetilde{T}\coloneqq\|(E_{i}-E_{j})U\|^{2}
. In particular, we will show that
\widetilde{T}
, properly centered and scaled by a term of order
\rho_{n}r^{1/2}
, converges to a weighted sum of independent
\chi^{2}_{1}
random variables. As a by-product, we have
\|(E_{i}-E_{j})U\|=O_{p}(\rho_{n}^{1/2}r^{1/2})
, so that

\Bigl|\|\widehat{\xi}_{i}-\widehat{\xi}_{j}\|^{2}-\|(E_{i}-E_{j})U\|^{2}\Bigr|=o_{p}(\rho_{n}r^{1/2}).

Slutsky’s theorem implies the limiting distribution of
T\coloneqq\|\widehat{\xi}_{i}-\widehat{\xi}_{j}\|^{2}
is the same as that of
\widetilde{T}
, after centering and scaling by a term of order
\rho_{n}r^{1/2}
.

We now derive the limiting distribution for
\widetilde{T}
.
Recall
Eq.

4.8
and write

\widetilde{T}=\|(E_{i}-E_{j})U\|^{2}=\zeta^{\top}\mathcal{D}UU^{\top}\mathcal{D}\zeta,

where
\zeta=(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\dots,\zeta_{n})
is a vector whose components are independent sub-Gaussian random variables with mean zero and variance one.

Next, we apply the following comparison result for quadratic forms.

Theorem 6

(Quadratic forms comparison
[
48
]
)
.

Let
X_{1},X_{2},\dots,
be a sequence of independent random variables with

\mathbb{E}[X_{k}]=0,\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{Var}[X_{k}]=1\quad\text{for all $k$.}

(A.81)

Let
W(n)=\sum_{1\leq k<\ell\leq n}X_{k}X_{\ell}m_{k\ell}
be a quadratic form, where the coefficients
(m_{k\ell})
can vary with
n
. Suppose
M=[m_{k\ell}]
is symmetric, with zeroes on the diagonal, and

\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}m_{k\ell}^{2}=1.

Let
F=(F_{1},F_{2},\dots)
and
G=(G_{1},G_{2},\dots,)
be two arbitrary collections of cumulative distribution functions for random variables
(X_{1},X_{2},\dots)
satisfying
Eq.

A.81
. Denote by
Q(W(n),F)
and
Q(W(n),G)
the distribution of
W(n)
when the
(X_{1},X_{2},\dots)
has CDFs
F
and
G
, respectively. Let
\Psi_{k}=F_{k}-G_{k}
. Define

s_{k}^{2}(n)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}m_{k\ell}^{2}.

Now, suppose that, as
n\rightarrow\infty
, the following conditions are satisfied

1.

For any
A\rightarrow\infty
,

\sup_{1\leq k\leq n}\int_{|x|\geq A}x^{2}dG_{k}(x)\rightarrow 0.

(A.82)

2.

For any fixed but arbitrary
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{n}m_{k\ell}^{2}\iint_{|xy|\geq|\epsilon/m_{k\ell}|}|xy\Psi_{k}(x)\Psi_{\ell}(y)|\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y\rightarrow 0,

(A.83)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}s_{k}^{2}(n)\int_{|x|\geq\epsilon/s_{k}(n)}|x\Psi_{k}(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x\rightarrow 0.

(A.84)

Then,
Q(W(n),F)\rightarrow Q(W(n),G)
in distribution as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Recall that we denote
d_{k}^{2}=2p_{ik}(1-p_{ik})
. We now verify the conditions in

Theorem

6
for the case when the
F_{k}
’s are the CDFs for discrete random variables with probability mass function

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(X_{k}=0)=1-d_{k}^{2}=p_{ik}^{2}+(1-p_{ik})^{2},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(X_{k}=-1/d_{k})=\mathbb{P}(X_{k}=1/d_{k})=p_{ik}(1-p_{ik})=\frac{1}{2}d_{k}^{2}

and
G_{k}\equiv\Phi
for all
k
where
\Phi
is the CDF of the standard normal distribution.

Eq.

A.82
is straightforward to verify. Indeed,
x^{2}e^{-x^{2}/2}\precsim x^{-2}
as
x
increases and hence

\lim_{A\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{1\leq k\leq n}\int_{|x|\geq A}x^{2}dG_{k}(x)\precsim\lim_{A\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{1\leq k\leq n}\int_{|x|\geq A}x^{-2}\,\mathrm{d}x\precsim\lim_{A\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{1\leq k\leq n}A^{-1}=0.

We next verify the conditions in
Eqs.

A.83
and
A.84
. Define

M^{*}=\mathcal{D}UU^{\top}\mathcal{D},\quad M^{(0)}=M^{*}-\mathrm{diag}(M^{*}),\quad M=\frac{1}{\|M^{(0)}\|_{F}}M^{(0)}.

Here,
M^{(0)}
is obtained by setting the diagonal entries of
M^{*}
to zero while
\|M\|_{F}=1
.
Let
m^{*}_{k\ell}
denote the
k\ell
-th entry of
M^{*}
. From the above criteria for
r
, we have

\displaystyle\|M^{*}\|_{F}^{2}=\mathrm{tr}\bigl(U^{\top}\mathcal{D}^{2}U\bigr)^{2}\asymp\rho_{n}^{2}r,

(A.85)

\displaystyle\max_{k}|m_{kk}^{*}|\leq\bigl(\|\mathcal{D}\|^{2}\times\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\bigr)\leq\frac{4\rho_{n}^{2}}{|\lambda_{r}|},

(A.86)

\displaystyle\|M^{(0)}\|_{F}^{2}=\|M^{*}\|_{F}^{2}-\sum_{k}(m_{kk}^{*})^{2}\leq\|M^{*}\|_{F}^{2}-\frac{16n\rho_{n}^{4}}{\lambda_{r}^{2}}=(1-o(1))\|M^{*}\|_{F}^{2}.

(A.87)

The quadratic form
\zeta^{\top}\mathcal{D}UU^{\top}\mathcal{D}\zeta
in
Eq.

4.8
can be written as

\zeta^{\top}M^{*}\zeta=\zeta^{\top}M^{(0)}\zeta+\sum_{k}\zeta_{k}^{2}m_{kk}^{*}=\|M^{(0)}\|_{F}\times\zeta^{\top}M\zeta+\sum_{k}\zeta_{k}^{2}m_{kk}^{*}.

(A.88)

For the condition in
Eq.

A.84
, let
m_{kk}^{(2)}
and
m_{kk}^{(2*)}
denote the
k
-th diagonal element of
M^{2}
and
(M^{*})^{2}
.
Then,

\max_{k}m_{kk}^{(2*)}\leq\|(M^{*})^{2}\|_{\max}=\|\mathcal{D}UU^{\top}\mathcal{D}^{2}UU^{\top}\mathcal{D}\|_{\max}\leq\|\mathcal{D}U\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|U^{\top}\mathcal{D}^{2}U\|\times\|\mathcal{D}U\|_{2\to\infty},

where the first inequality follows from
\|MN^{\top}\|_{\max}\leq\|M\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|N\|_{2\to\infty}
together with
Eq.

1.1
. As
\mathcal{D}
is a diagonal matrix, we have
\|\mathcal{D}U\|_{2\to\infty}\leq\|\mathcal{D}\|\times\|U\|_{2\to\infty}
. Hence,

\begin{split}s_{k}^{2}(n)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}m_{k\ell}^{2}\leq m_{kk}^{(2)}&\leq\frac{1}{\|M_{0}\|_{F}^{2}}\times m_{kk}^{(2*)}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\|M_{0}\|_{F}^{2}}\times\|\mathcal{D}\|^{2}\times\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\times\|U^{\top}\mathcal{D}^{2}U\|\\
&\lesssim(\rho_{n}^{2}r)^{-1}\times\rho_{n}\times n\rho_{n}^{2}\times\lambda_{r}^{-2}\times\rho_{n}\lesssim r^{-1}\lambda_{r}^{-2}n\rho_{n}^{2}.\end{split}

Now, choose an arbitrary but fixed
\epsilon>0
. Then,

\epsilon/s_{k}(n)\gtrsim r^{1/2}|\lambda_{r}|(n^{-1/2}\rho_{n}^{-1})\epsilon=\omega(\rho_{n}^{-1/2})=\omega(1/d_{kk}),

as our criterion in
Eq.

A.80
implies
\lambda_{r}=\omega((n\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2})
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. We thus have
F_{k}(-\epsilon/s_{k}(n))=0
and
F_{k}(\epsilon/s_{k}(n))=1
for sufficiently large
n
, hence

\begin{split}\int_{|x|\geq\epsilon/s_{k}(n)}|x\Psi_{k}(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x&=\int_{x\leq-\epsilon/s_{k}(n)}|x\Phi(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{x\geq\epsilon/s_{k}(n)}|x(1-\Phi(x))|\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&=2\int_{x\geq\epsilon/s_{k}(n)}x(1-\Phi(x))\,\mathrm{d}x.\end{split}

Next, recall the Mill’s ratio upper bound

\frac{1-\Phi(x)}{\phi(x)}\leq\frac{1}{x},

where
\phi(x)
is the probability density function of a standard normal random variable. We then have

\int_{x\geq\epsilon/s_{k}(n)}x(1-\Phi(x))\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\int_{x\geq\epsilon/s_{k}(n)}\phi(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=1-\Phi\Bigl(\frac{\epsilon}{s_{k}(n)}\Bigr)\leq\frac{\phi(\epsilon/s_{k}(n))}{\epsilon/s_{k}(n)},

(A.89)

and hence

\begin{split}\sum_{k=1}^{n}s_{k}^{2}(n)\int_{|x|\geq\epsilon/s_{k}(n)}|x\Psi_{k}(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x&\leq 2\sum_{k=1}^{n}\epsilon^{-1}s_{k}^{3}(n)\phi(\epsilon/s_{k}(n))\\
&\leq 2n\epsilon^{-1}\bigl(\max_{k}s_{k}^{3}(n)\phi(\epsilon/s_{k}(n))\bigr)\\
&\precsim\epsilon^{-1}\bigl(\max_{k}s_{k}^{3}(n)\bigr)\times\bigl(\max_{k}e^{\log(n)-\epsilon^{2}/(2s_{k}^{2}(n))}\bigr).\end{split}

(A.90)

As
\epsilon^{2}/s_{k}^{2}(n)\gtrsim r\lambda_{r}^{2}(n\rho_{n}^{-2})\epsilon^{2}
and
\epsilon>0
is fixed but arbitrary, we have

\log(n)-\epsilon^{2}/(2s_{k}^{2}(n))\leq 0

as
n
increases (see
Eq.

A.80
. We thus have

\sum_{k=1}^{n}s_{k}^{2}(n)\int_{|x|\geq\epsilon/s_{k}(n)}|x\Psi(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x\precsim\epsilon^{-1}\times\max_{k}s_{k}^{3}(n)\precsim\epsilon^{-1}(r^{-1}\lambda_{r}^{-1}n^{1/2}\rho_{n})^{3/2}\rightarrow 0

as
n\rightarrow\infty
.
Eq.

A.84
is therefore satisfied.

For
Eq.

A.83
, we have

\begin{split}\max_{k\ell}m_{k\ell}&\leq\frac{1}{\|M^{(0)}\|_{F}}\times\|M^{*}\|_{\max}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\|M^{(0)}\|_{F}}\times\|\mathcal{D}\|^{2}\times\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\rho_{n}r^{1/2}}\times\rho_{n}\times\frac{n\rho_{n}^{2}}{\lambda_{r}^{2}}\lesssim\frac{n\rho_{n}^{2}}{r^{1/2}\lambda_{r}^{2}}.\end{split}

Now let
c_{k\ell}=(|\epsilon/m_{k\ell}|)^{1/2}
, and write
h(x,y)=|xy\Psi_{k}(x)\Psi_{\ell}(y)|
. Define the regions

\displaystyle S_{1}=\{(x,y)\colon|x|\geq c_{k\ell},|y|\leq c_{k\ell}\},\quad S_{2}=\{(x,y)\colon|x|\leq c_{k\ell},|y|\geq c_{k\ell}\},\quad S_{3}=\{|x|\geq c_{k\ell},|y|\geq c_{k\ell}\},

and note that
S\coloneqq\{(x,y)\colon|xy|\geq|\epsilon/m_{k\ell}|\}\subset S_{1}\cup S_{2}\cup S_{3}
. We then have

\begin{split}\iint_{S}h(x,y)\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y&\leq\iint_{S_{1}}h(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{S_{2}}h(x,y)\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x+\iint_{S_{3}}h(x,y)\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq\int_{|x|\leq c_{k\ell}}|x\Psi_{k}(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x\int_{|y|\geq c_{k\ell}}|y\Psi_{\ell}(y)|\,\mathrm{d}y+\int_{|x|\geq c_{k\ell}}|x\Psi_{k}(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x\int_{|y|\leq c_{k\ell}}|y\Psi_{\ell}(y)|\,\mathrm{d}y\\
&+\int_{|x|\geq c_{k\ell}}|x\Psi_{k}(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x\int_{|y|\geq c_{k\ell}}|y\Psi_{\ell}(y)|\,\mathrm{d}y.\end{split}

Now,
c_{k\ell}\succsim\epsilon^{1/2}r^{1/4}|\lambda_{r}|/(n^{1/2}\rho_{n})=\omega(\rho_{n}^{-1/2})

uniformly
for all
\{k,\ell\}
provided that
Eq.

A.80
is satisfied. Thus, by a similar argument to that for deriving
Eq.

A.89
, we have

\int_{|x|\geq c_{k\ell}}|x\Psi_{k}(x)|\mathrm{d}x\leq\frac{2}{c_{k\ell}}\phi(c_{k\ell})

for all
\{k,\ell\}
, and similarly for
\int_{|y|\geq c_{k\ell}}|y\Psi_{\ell}(y)|\mathrm{d}y
.
We therefore have

\begin{split}\iint_{S}h(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y&\leq\frac{2}{c_{k\ell}}\phi(c_{k\ell})\Bigl(\int_{|x|\leq c_{k\ell}}|x\Psi_{k}(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{|y|\leq c_{k\ell}}|y\Psi_{\ell}(y)|\,\mathrm{d}y\Bigr)+\frac{4}{c_{k\ell}^{2}}\phi^{2}(c_{k\ell})\\
&\leq 2c_{k\ell}\phi(c_{k\ell})+\frac{4}{c_{k\ell}^{2}}\phi^{2}(c_{k\ell}).\end{split}

Finally, as
m_{k\ell}^{2}\lesssim n^{2}\rho_{n}^{4}/(r\lambda_{r}^{4})
for all
\{k,\ell\}
, we have

\begin{split}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}m_{k\ell}^{2}\int_{|xy|\geq|\epsilon/m_{k\ell}}h(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y&\precsim\frac{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{4}}{r\lambda_{r}^{4}}\max_{k\ell}\Bigl(c_{k\ell}\phi(c_{k\ell})+\frac{1}{c_{k\ell}^{2}}\phi^{2}(c_{k\ell})\Bigr)\rightarrow 0\end{split}

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. The condition in
Eq.

A.83
is therefore satisfied.

Now,
\zeta_{k}^{2}=d_{k}^{-2}Y_{k}
, where the
\{Y_{k}\}
are independent Bernoulli random variables with success probabilities
\{d_{k}^{2}\}
. We thus have

\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{k}^{2}]=1,\quad\mathrm{Var}[\zeta_{kk}^{2}]=\frac{d_{k}^{2}(1-d_{k}^{2})}{d_{k}^{4}}=\frac{1-d_{k}^{2}}{d_{k}^{2}}.

Therefore, by Bernstein’s inequality, we have

\Bigl|\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\zeta_{k}^{2}-1)m_{kk}^{*}\Bigr|\precsim\Bigl(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{1-d_{k}^{2}}{d_{k}^{2}}(m_{kk}^{*})^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\times\log^{1/2}{n}\precsim\frac{n^{1/2}\rho_{n}^{3/2}\log^{1/2}{n}}{|\lambda_{r}|}

with high probability, and hence

\frac{1}{\|M_{0}\|_{F}}\Bigl|\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\zeta_{k}^{2}-1)m_{kk}^{*}\Bigr|\precsim\frac{(n\rho_{n}\log n)^{1/2}}{r^{1/2}|\lambda_{r}|}\rightarrow 0

almost surely. We therefore have

\frac{\zeta^{\top}M^{*}\zeta-\sum_{k}m_{kk}^{*}}{\|M_{0}\|_{F}}=\zeta^{\top}M\zeta+\frac{1}{\|M_{0}\|_{F}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\zeta_{k}^{2}-1)m_{kk}^{*}=\zeta^{\top}M\zeta+o_{P}(1),

and hence, by applying
Theorem

6
, we have

\frac{\zeta^{\top}M^{*}\zeta-\sum_{k}m_{kk}^{*}}{\|M_{0}\|_{F}}=Z^{\top}MZ+o_{P}(1)=\sum_{s\geq 1}Z_{s}^{2}\lambda_{s}(M)+o_{P}(1),

where
Z=(Z_{1},Z_{2},\dots)
is a vector of independent
N(0,1)
random variables and
\lambda_{s}(M)
are the eigenvalues of the matrix
M
ordered in decreasing magnitudes. As the diagonal entries of
M
are all zeroes and
\|M\|_{F}^{2}=1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{s\geq 1}Z_{s}^{2}\lambda_{s}(M)\right]=\sum_{s\geq 1}\mathbb{E}[Z_{s}^{2}]\lambda_{s}(M)=\sum_{s\geq 1}\lambda_{s}(M)=0,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left[\sum_{s\geq 1}Z_{s}^{2}\lambda_{s}(M)\right]=\sum_{s\geq 1}\mathrm{Var}[Z_{s}^{2}]\times\lambda_{s}^{2}(M)=2\|M\|_{F}^{2}=2.

Next, by Weyl’s inequality, we have

\begin{split}\max_{s}\Bigl|\lambda_{s}(M)-\frac{1}{\|M_{0}\|_{F}}\lambda_{s}(M^{*})\Bigr|&\leq\frac{1}{\|M_{0}\|_{F}}\max_{k}m_{kk}^{*}\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{\rho_{n}r^{1/2}}\times\frac{\rho_{n}^{2}}{|\lambda_{r}|}\lesssim\frac{\rho_{n}}{r^{1/2}|\lambda_{r}|}.\end{split}

Note that
M^{*}=\mathcal{D}UU^{\top}\mathcal{D}
is of rank
r
and the non-zero eigenvalues of
M^{*}
are the same as those of
U^{\top}\mathcal{D}^{2}U
and hence of magnitude
\rho_{n}
. We thus have

|\lambda_{s}(M)|\asymp r^{-1/2}\quad\text{for $1\leq s\leq r$},\quad|\lambda_{s}(M)|\precsim\frac{\rho_{n}}{r^{1/2}|\lambda_{r}|},\qquad\text{for $s\geq r+1$}.

Next, define
Y^{(r)}=\tfrac{1}{\|M_{0}\|_{F}}\sum_{s=1}^{r}(Z_{s}^{2}-1)\lambda_{s}(M^{*})
. Then,
\mathbb{E}[Y^{(r)}]=0=\mathbb{E}[\sum_{s\geq 1}Z_{s}^{2}\lambda_{s}(M)]
. Furthermore, we also have

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[Y^{(r)}-\sum_{s\geq 1}Z_{s}^{2}\lambda_{s}(M)\Bigr]^{2}&=\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\sum_{s=1}^{r}Z_{s}^{2}\Bigl(\lambda_{s}(M)-\frac{\lambda_{s}(M^{*})}{\|M_{0}\|_{F}}\Bigr)+\sum_{s\geq r+1}Z_{s}^{2}\lambda_{s}(M)\Bigr]^{2}\\
&=2\Bigl(\sum_{s=1}^{r}\Bigl(\lambda_{s}(M)-\frac{\lambda_{s}(M^{*})}{\|M_{0}\|_{F}}\Bigr)^{2}+\sum_{s\geq r+1}\lambda_{s}^{2}(M)\Bigr)\\
&\lesssim\frac{n\rho_{n}^{2}}{r\lambda_{r}^{2}}.\end{split}

(A.91)

Therefore, as
n\rightarrow\infty
, we have

Y^{(r)}-\sum_{s\geq 1}Z_{s}^{2}\lambda_{s}(M)\rightarrow 0

in probability.
In summary, we have under the null hypothesis of
X_{i}=X_{j}
that

\frac{\|(A\widehat{U})_{i}-(A\widehat{U})_{j}\|^{2}-\mathrm{tr}\,M^{*}}{\|M_{0}\|_{F}}\rightsquigarrow\frac{1}{\|M_{0}\|_{F}}\sum_{s=1}^{r}(Z_{s}^{2}-1)\lambda_{s}(M^{*}).

(A.92)

Recalling
Eq.

A.87
, we have by Slutsky’s theorem that

\frac{\|(A\widehat{U})_{i}-(A\widehat{U})_{j}\|^{2}-\mathrm{tr}\,M^{*}}{\|M^{*}\|_{F}}\rightsquigarrow\frac{1}{\|M^{*}\|_{F}}\sum_{s=1}^{r}(Z_{s}^{2}-1)\lambda_{s}(M^{*}).

(A.93)

Finally, if
\kappa
has infinite rank, then we can choose
r\rightarrow\infty
as
n\rightarrow\infty
such that, by the Lindeberg–Feller central limit theorem (see Theorem 27.2 and Problem 27.6 in
[
8
]
), we have

\frac{1}{\|M^{*}\|_{F}}\sum_{s=1}^{r}(Z_{s}^{2}-1)\lambda_{s}(M^{*})\rightsquigarrow N(0,2).

This concludes the proof of
Theorem

4

A.9
Proof of
Lemma

1

First, consider the expansion

\widehat{\theta}-\mathrm{tr}M^{*}=\mathrm{tr}\,(\widehat{U}^{\top}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}\widehat{U}-U^{\top}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}U)+\mathrm{tr}\,(U^{\top}(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}-\mathcal{D}^{2})U).

Recall that
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}
is a diagonal matrix with
\widehat{d}_{k}\in\{0,1\}
for all
k
. Now, let
S=\{k\colon\widehat{d}_{k}=1\}
and
\zeta_{2,\infty}=\|\widehat{U}W-U\|_{2\to\infty}
where
W=W^{(n)}
is the orthogonal matrix appearing in
Theorem

3
. If we select
r
according to the criterion in
Eq.

4.11

then

\zeta_{2,\infty}\lesssim\frac{\rho_{n}^{1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{|\lambda_{r}|}

with high probability (see
Eq.

3.31
)
Also,
\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\leq|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}n^{1/2}\rho_{n}
(see
Eq.

A.45
).

We therefore have

\begin{split}\Bigl|\mathrm{tr}\,(\widehat{U}^{\top}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}\widehat{U}-U^{\top}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}U)\Bigr|&=\Bigl|\sum_{k\in S}(\|\widehat{U}_{k}\|^{2}-\|U_{k}\|^{2})\Bigr|\\
&\leq\bigl(2\zeta_{2,\infty}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}+\zeta_{2,\infty}^{2}\bigr)\times|S|\\
&\lesssim\frac{n^{1/2}\rho_{n}^{3/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{\lambda_{r}^{2}}\times|S|\\
&\precsim\frac{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{5/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{\lambda_{r}^{2}}\end{split}

(A.94)

with high probability, and final inequality follows from Chernoff bound and the fact that
\widehat{d}_{k}
are independent Bernoulli random variables with success probabilities
d_{k}^{2}=2p_{ik}(1-p_{ik})\lesssim\rho_{n}
. For the term
U^{\top}(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}-\mathcal{D}^{2})U
we have

\mathrm{tr}\,(U^{\top}(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}-\mathcal{D}^{2})U)=\sum_{k}\|U_{k}\|^{2}\times\bigl((a_{ik}-a_{jk})^{2}-d_{k}^{2}\bigr).

Now,
\mathbb{E}[(a_{ik}-a_{jk})^{2}]=d_{k}^{2}
and
\mathrm{Var}[(a_{ik}-a_{jk})^{2}]=d_{k}^{2}(1-d_{k}^{2})\precsim\rho_{n}
. Therefore, by Bernstein’s inequality, we have

\sum_{k}\|U_{k}\|^{2}\bigl((a_{ik}-a_{jk})^{2}-d_{k}^{2}\bigr)\precsim(n\rho_{n})^{1/2}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\log^{1/2}{n}\lesssim\frac{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{5/2}\log^{1/2}{n}}{\lambda_{r}^{2}}

(A.95)

with high probability. Combining
Eqs.

A.85
,
A.94
and
A.95
we obtain

\frac{\widehat{\theta}-\mathrm{tr}\,M^{*}}{\|M^{*}\|_{F}}\lesssim\frac{(n\rho_{n})^{3/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{r^{1/2}\lambda_{r}^{2}}\rightarrow 0

as claimed. For the term
\widehat{\sigma}
, first note that

\begin{split}\bigl|\widehat{\sigma}-\|M^{*}\|_{F}\bigr|&=\bigl|\|\widehat{U}^{\top}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}\widehat{U}\|_{F}-\|U^{\top}\mathcal{D}^{2}U\|_{F}\bigr|\\
&=\bigl|\|W^{\top}\widehat{U}^{\top}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}\widehat{U}W\|_{F}-\|U^{\top}\mathcal{D}^{2}U\|_{F}\bigr|\\
&\leq\|W^{\top}\widehat{U}^{\top}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}\widehat{U}W-U^{\top}\mathcal{D}^{2}U\|_{F}\\
&\leq\|W^{\top}\widehat{U}^{\top}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}\widehat{U}W-U^{\top}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}U\|_{F}+\|U^{\top}(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}-\mathcal{D}^{2})U\|_{F}\\
&\leq\|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}(\widehat{U}W-U)\|_{F}^{2}+2\|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}(\widehat{U}W-U)\|_{F}\cdot\|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}U\|_{F}+\|U^{\top}(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}-\mathcal{D}^{2})U\|_{F}.\end{split}

Following a similar argument to that for
Eq.

A.94
, we have

\begin{split}\|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}(\widehat{U}W-U)\|_{F}&\leq|S|^{1/2}\times\|\widehat{U}W-U\|_{2\to\infty}\\
&\lesssim\frac{\rho_{n}^{1/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{|\lambda_{r}|}\times|S|^{1/2}\\
&\lesssim\frac{n^{1/2}\rho_{n}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{|\lambda_{r}|}\end{split}

(A.96)

with high probability. Furthermore, we also have

\|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}U\|_{F}\leq|S|^{1/2}\times\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\leq|S|^{1/2}\times n^{1/2}\rho_{n}|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}\lesssim n\rho_{n}^{3/2}|\lambda_{r}|^{-1}

with high probability.
Combining the above bounds gives

\begin{split}\|W^{\top}\widehat{U}^{\top}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}\widehat{U}W-U^{\top}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}U\|_{F}&\leq\|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}(\widehat{U}W-U)\|_{F}^{2}+2\|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}(\widehat{U}W-U)\|_{F}\cdot\|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}U\|_{F}\\
&\lesssim\frac{n\rho_{n}^{2}(r+\log n)+n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{5/2}(r^{1/2}+\log^{1/2}{n})}{\lambda_{r}^{2}}\end{split}

(A.97)

with high probability.

Next, we note that
\|U^{\top}(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}-\mathcal{D}^{2})U\|_{F}\leq r\|U^{\top}(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}-\mathcal{D}^{2})U\|_{\max}
. Furthermore, the
rs
-th entry of
U^{\top}(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}-\mathcal{D}^{2})U
can be written as

\sum_{k}u_{rk}u_{sk}[(a_{ik}-a_{jk})^{2}-d_{k}^{2}],

where
u_{rk}
is the
rk
-th entry of
U
. The above is, conditioned on
P
, also a sum of independent mean zero random variables. Hence, by another application of Bernstein’s inequality, we have

\begin{split}\sum_{k}u_{rk}u_{sk}[(a_{ik}-a_{jk})^{2}-d_{k}^{2}]&\lesssim(n\rho_{n})^{1/2}\times\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\times\log^{1/2}{n}\\
&\lesssim\frac{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{5/2}\log^{1/2}{n}}{\lambda_{r}^{2}}\end{split}

(A.98)

with high probability, and thus

\begin{split}\|\widehat{U}^{\top}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}\widehat{U}-U^{\top}\mathcal{D}^{2}U\|_{F}\lesssim\frac{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{5/2}r\log^{1/2}{n}}{\lambda_{r}^{2}}\end{split}

(A.99)

with high probability. Combining
Eqs.

A.97
and
A.99
we obtain

\|\widehat{U}^{\top}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}\widehat{U}-U^{\top}\mathcal{D}^{2}U\|_{F}\lesssim\frac{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{5/2}r\log^{1/2}{n}}{\lambda_{r}^{2}}.

(A.100)

Eq.

A.100
then implies

\begin{split}\frac{\|W^{\top}\widehat{U}^{\top}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{2}\widehat{U}W-U^{\top}\mathcal{D}^{2}U\|_{F}}{\|M_{*}\|_{F}}&\lesssim\frac{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{5/2}r\log^{1/2}{n}}{\lambda_{r}^{2}}\times\frac{1}{\rho_{n}\sqrt{r}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{(n\rho_{n})^{3/2}(r\log n)^{1/2}}{\lambda_{r}^{2}}\overset{\mathrm{p}}{\longrightarrow}0\end{split}

(A.101)

as
n\rightarrow\infty
, provided that
r
is chosen according to the criteria in
Theorem

4
.
Eq.

A.101
also guarantees

\widehat{\sigma}/\|M_{*}\|_{F}\rightarrow 1
in
probability as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Appendix B
Technical lemmas

We first recall a version of Bernstein’s inequality for bounded random variables.

Lemma 3

(Theorem 2.8.4 in
[
58
]
)
.

Let
X_{1},X_{2},\dots,X_{m}
be independent mean zero random variables. Suppose there exists a constant
M>0
such that
|X_{i}|\leq M
almost surely for all
1\leq i\leq m
. Let
\sigma^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]
. Then, for every
t>0
, it holds that

\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\bigl|\sum_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}\bigr|\geq t\Bigr)\leq 2\exp\left(\frac{-t^{2}/2}{\sigma^{2}+Mt/3}\right).

(B.1)

In particular, for any
\nu>0
,

\bigl|\sum_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}\bigr|\leq\sqrt{2\nu}\sigma(\log n)^{1/2}+\frac{2}{3}\nu M\log n

(B.2)

with probability at least
1-2n^{-\nu}
.

The next lemma provides a high-probability bound for the spectral norm of
E
and follows by adapting the results in
[
5
]
to the setting of the current paper.

Lemma 4
.

Assume the setting in
Theorem

1
or
Theorem

2
. Then, for any
\alpha\geq 3
and
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|E\|\geq e^{2/\alpha}(2\sqrt{2}\sigma+14\alpha\sqrt{\log n})+t\bigr)\leq\exp(-t^{2}/2),

(B.3)

where
\sigma^{2}=\max_{i}\sum_{j}p_{ij}(1-p_{ij})
.

Proof of
Lemma

4
.

First, note that the spectral norm of a matrix is a separately convex function of its entries. As the entries of
E
are independent random variables bounded in magnitude
1
,
[
11
, Theorem 6.10]
yields

\mathbb{P}(\|E\|\geq\mathbb{E}[\|E\|]+t)\leq\exp(-t^{2}/2).

Next, let
A^{\prime}
be an independent copy of
A
, so that
E^{\prime}=A^{\prime}-P
is an independent copy of
E
. Then,
\mathbb{E}[\|E\|]\leq\mathbb{E}[\|E-E^{\prime}\|]
by Jensen’s inequality, where we had used the fact that
\mathbb{E}[E^{\prime}]=0
. Now, the upper triangular entries of
E-E^{\prime}
are independent,
symmetric
random variables (i.e.,
E_{ij}-E_{ij}^{\prime}
has the same distribution as
E_{ij}^{\prime}-E_{ij}
) bounded in absolute value by
1
. Therefore, by
[
5
, Corollary 3.6]
, for any
\alpha\geq 3
,

\mathbb{E}[\|E-E^{\prime}\|]\leq e^{2/\alpha}(2\widetilde{\sigma}+14\alpha\sqrt{\log n}),

where
\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}=\max_{i}\sum_{j}\mathrm{Var}[(E_{ij}-E_{ij}^{\prime})]
. Straightforward calculations yield

\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}=\max_{i}\sum_{j}2p_{ij}(1-p_{ij})(1-2p_{ij}(1-p_{ij}))\leq 2\max_{i}\sum_{j}p_{ij}(1-p_{ij}).

Eq.

B.3
follows directly from combining the above bounds. This completes the proof of
Lemma

4
.
∎

Taking
\alpha=4
in
Lemma

4
and applying
\sigma^{2}\leq n\rho_{n}
, it holds for any
\nu\geq 0
that

\mathbb{P}(\|E\|\geq 2\sqrt{2en\rho_{n}}+(56\sqrt{e}+\sqrt{2\nu})\sqrt{\log n})\leq n^{-\nu}.

(B.4)

Next, we present a collection of lemmas for bounding the quantities
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
and
\psi_{3}^{(k)}
in the proofs of
Theorem

1
through
Theorem

3
.

Lemma 5
.

Assume the setting in
Theorem

1
or
Theorem

2
. Let
U
be a
n\times r
matrix not depending on
E
with
U^{\top}U=I
. Then,

\displaystyle\|U^{\top}EU\|\leq 4\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}+\frac{8}{3}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\vartheta(\nu,r,n),

(B.5)

\displaystyle\|U^{\top}E\widehat{U}\|\leq 4\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}+\frac{8}{3}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)+\frac{2\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}},

(B.6)

with probability at least
1-2n^{-\nu}
, where
\vartheta(\nu,r,n)=\nu\log n+r\log 9
.

Proof of
Lemma

5
.

First, we shall bound
\|U^{\top}EU\|
using an
\epsilon
-net argument. By the definition of the operator norm,

\|U^{\top}EU\|=\max_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{p}\colon\|x\|=1}|x^{\top}U^{\top}EUx|.

Now, fix
x\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
,
\|x\|=1
, and let
\xi=Ux
, so

x^{\top}U^{\top}EUx=2\sum_{i<j}e_{ij}\xi_{i}\xi_{j}+\sum_{i}e_{ii}\xi_{i}^{2}.

Letting
u_{i}
denote the
i
-th row of
U
, we have
\max_{i}|\xi_{i}|\leq\max_{i}\|u_{i}\|\times\|x\|\leq\|U\|_{2\to\infty}
. In particular,
\sum_{i<j}e_{ij}\xi_{i}\xi_{j}
is a sum of independent mean zero random variables satisfying

2\max_{i,j}|e_{ij}\xi_{i}\xi_{j}|\leq 2\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}.

Define
\sigma^{2}=\sum_{i<j}\mathrm{Var}[2e_{ij}\xi_{i}\xi_{j}]+\sum_{i}\mathrm{Var}[e_{ii}\xi_{i}^{2}]
. Since
\|\xi\|=\|Ux\|=\|x\|=1
, we have

\begin{split}\sigma^{2}&=4\sum_{i<j}p_{ij}(1-p_{ij})\xi_{i}^{2}\xi_{j}^{2}+\sum_{i}p_{ii}(1-p_{ii})\xi_{i}^{4}\leq 2\sum_{i}\sum_{j}p_{ij}(1-p_{ij})\xi_{i}^{2}\xi_{j}^{2}\leq 2\rho_{n}.\end{split}

Applying Bernstein’s inequality therefore yields

\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\bigl|x^{\top}U^{\top}EUx\bigr|\geq t\Bigr)\leq 2\exp\Bigl(\frac{-t^{2}}{2\rho_{n}+2\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}t/3}\Bigr).

Now, let
\mathcal{S}_{1/4}
be a minimal
1/4
-net for the unit ball in
\mathbb{R}^{r}
with
\epsilon\in(0,1)
. Thus
|\mathcal{S}_{\epsilon}|\leq 9^{r}
, for example, by
[
58
, Corollary 4.2.13]
. Next, let
x_{*}
satisfy
|x_{*}^{\top}U^{\top}EUx_{*}|=\|U^{\top}EU\|
and
\|x_{*}\|=1
, so there exists a vector
z\in\mathcal{S}_{1/4}
such that
\|z-x_{*}\|\leq 1/4
. Furthermore,

\begin{split}|x_{*}^{\top}U^{\top}EUx_{*}|&=|(x_{*}-z)^{\top}U^{\top}EUx_{*}+z^{\top}U^{\top}EU(x_{*}-z)+z^{\top}U^{\top}EUz|\\
&\leq|z^{\top}U^{\top}EUz|+\|z-x_{*}\|\times(\|U^{\top}EUx_{*}\|+\|U^{\top}EUz\|)\\
&\leq|z^{\top}U^{\top}EUz|+\frac{1}{2}\|U^{\top}EU\|.\end{split}

Hence, for all
t>0
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}[\|U^{\top}EU\|\geq 2t]&\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigl[\sup_{z\in\mathcal{S}_{1/2}}|z^{\top}U^{\top}EUz|\geq t\Bigr]\\
&\leq 2|\mathcal{S}_{1/4}|\exp\Bigl(\frac{-t^{2}}{4\rho_{n}+4\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}t/3}\Bigr)\\
&\leq 2\exp\Bigl(r\log(9)-\frac{t^{2}}{4\rho_{n}+4\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}t/3}\Bigr).\end{split}

Define
\vartheta(\nu,r,n)=\nu\log n+r\log 9
, and let

t_{*}=2\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}+\frac{4}{3}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\vartheta(\nu,r,n).

(B.7)

We then have

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|U^{\top}EU\|\geq 2t_{*}\bigr)\leq 2n^{-\nu},\end{split}

which yields the stated bound for
\|U^{\top}EU\|
as desired.

The stated bound for
U^{\top}E\widehat{U}
follows directly from the triangle inequality and the Davis–Kahan theorem, namely

\begin{split}\|U^{\top}E\widehat{U}\|&\leq\|U^{\top}EUU^{\top}\widehat{U}\|+\|U^{\top}E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|\\
&\leq\|U^{\top}EU\|+\|E\|\times\|(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|\\
&\leq\|U^{\top}EU\|+\frac{2\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}}.\end{split}

This completes the proof of
Lemma

5
.
∎

Lemma 6
.

Assume the setting in
Theorem

2
. Let
T=|\Lambda|^{1/2}JU^{\top}\widehat{U}-U^{\top}\widehat{U}|\widehat{\Lambda}|^{1/2}J
.
Then,

\displaystyle\|T\|\leq|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}\Bigl(8\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}+\frac{16}{3}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)+\frac{4\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}}\Bigr)

(B.8)

with probability at least
1-2n^{-\nu}
. Furthermore, under the setting in
Theorem

1
where
\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{2}\geq\dots\geq\lambda_{r}>0
then, for
T=\Lambda^{1/2}U^{\top}\widehat{U}-U^{\top}\widehat{U}\widehat{\Lambda}^{1/2}
,
Eq.

B.8
can be improved to

\displaystyle\|T\|\leq\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}\Bigl(4\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}+\frac{8}{3}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)+\frac{2\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}}\Bigr)

(B.9)

with probability at least
1-2n^{-\nu}
.

Proof of
Lemma

6
.

First, we collect several observations. Suppose that
t_{ij}
, the
ij
-th entry of
T
, is associated with
\lambda_{i}\geq 0
and
\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\geq 0
. Then,

t_{ij}=u_{i}^{\top}\widehat{u}_{j}(\lambda_{i}^{1/2}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}^{1/2})=\frac{u_{i}^{\top}\widehat{u}_{j}(\lambda_{i}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j})}{\widehat{\lambda}_{i}^{1/2}+\widehat{\lambda}_{j}^{1/2}}=\frac{u_{i}^{\top}(A-P)\widehat{u}_{j}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{i}^{1/2}+\widehat{\lambda}_{j}^{1/2}}=\frac{u_{i}^{\top}(A-P)\widehat{u}_{j}}{|\lambda_{i}|^{1/2}+|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|^{1/2}}.

Similarly, if
t_{ij}
is associated with
\lambda_{i}\leq 0
and
\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\leq 0
. then

t_{ij}=u_{i}^{\top}\widehat{u}_{j}(|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|^{1/2}-|\lambda_{i}|^{1/2})=\frac{u_{i}^{\top}\widehat{u}_{j}(|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|-|\lambda_{i}|)}{|\lambda_{i}|^{1/2}+|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|^{1/2}}=\frac{u_{i}^{\top}(A-P)\widehat{u}_{j}}{|\lambda_{i}|^{1/2}+|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|^{1/2}}.

Next, if
t_{ij}
is associated with
\lambda_{i}\geq 0
and
\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\leq 0
, then

\begin{split}t_{ij}&=u_{i}^{\top}\widehat{u}_{j}(\lambda_{i}^{1/2}+|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|^{1/2})\\
&=u_{i}^{\top}\widehat{u}_{j}\Bigl(\frac{|\lambda_{i}|+|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|+2|\lambda_{i}|^{1/2}\cdot|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|^{1/2}}{|\lambda_{i}|^{1/2}+|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|^{1/2}}\Bigr)\\
&=\frac{-u_{i}^{\top}(A-P)\widehat{u}_{j}}{|\lambda_{i}|^{1/2}+|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|^{1/2}}\Bigl(1+\frac{2|\lambda_{i}|^{1/2}\cdot|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|^{1/2}}{|\lambda_{i}|+|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|}\Bigr).\end{split}

Finally, if
t_{ij}
is associated with
\lambda_{i}\leq 0
and
\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\geq 0
, then

\begin{split}t_{ij}&=u_{i}^{\top}\widehat{u}_{j}(-|\lambda_{i}|^{1/2}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}^{1/2})\\
&=-u_{i}^{\top}\widehat{u}_{j}\Bigl(\frac{|\lambda_{i}|+|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|+2|\lambda_{i}|^{1/2}\cdot|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|^{1/2}}{|\lambda_{i}|^{1/2}+|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|^{1/2}}\Bigr)\\
&=\frac{u_{i}^{\top}(A-P)\widehat{u}_{j}}{|\lambda_{i}|^{1/2}+|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|^{1/2}}\Bigl(1+\frac{2|\lambda_{i}|^{1/2}\cdot|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|^{1/2}}{|\lambda_{i}|+|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|}\Bigr).\end{split}

Now, let
S
denote the
r\times r
matrix defined element-wise as
s_{ij}=1/(|\lambda_{i}|^{1/2}+|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|^{1/2})
, and let
H
denote the
r\times r
matrix with entries
h_{ij}=2(|\lambda_{i}|^{1/2}\cdot|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|^{1/2})/(|\lambda_{i}|+|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|)
.
As before, we write
E=(A-P)
.
Combining the above identities for
T
yields

\displaystyle T=(JU^{\top}E\widehat{U}J)\circ S+\mathcal{P}^{(1)}\circ S\circ H,\qquad\text{where}\quad\mathcal{P}^{(1)}=\begin{bmatrix}0&-U_{+}^{\top}E\widehat{U}_{-}\\
-U_{-}^{\top}E\widehat{U}_{+}&0\end{bmatrix},

and
\circ
denote the Hadamard (elementwise) matrix product. Next, define

\mathcal{P}^{(2)}=\begin{bmatrix}0&\,\,&\mathcal{P}^{(1)}\\
0&\,\,&0\end{bmatrix},

and let
\gamma=(|\lambda_{1}^{\downarrow}|,|\lambda_{2}^{\downarrow}|,\dots,|\lambda_{r}^{\downarrow}|,|\widehat{\lambda}_{1}^{\downarrow}|,|\widehat{\lambda}_{2}^{\downarrow}|,\dots,|\widehat{\lambda}_{r}^{\downarrow}|)
. Here,
\lambda_{i}^{\downarrow}
is a rearrangement of
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\dots\lambda_{r}
in decreasing value (recall that the
\lambda_{i}
are ordered in decreasing modulus), and similarly for
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}^{\downarrow}
.
Letting
\widetilde{S}
be the
2r\times 2r
matrix with entries
\widetilde{s}_{ij}=1/(\gamma_{i}+\gamma_{j})
and
\widetilde{H}
be the
2r\times 2r
matrix with entries
\widetilde{h}_{ij}=2\gamma_{i}^{1/2}\gamma_{j}^{1/2}/(\gamma_{i}+\gamma_{j})
, we have

\|\mathcal{P}^{(1)}\circ S\circ H\|=\|\mathcal{P}^{(2)}\circ\widetilde{S}\circ\widetilde{H}\|.

Here,
\widetilde{H}
is the Hadamard product of a positive semidefinite matrix with entries
2\gamma_{i}^{1/2}\gamma_{j}^{1/2}
and a
Cauchy matrix
with entries
1/(\gamma_{i}+\gamma_{j})
. Hence, by the Schur product formula
[
31
, Theorem 5.2.1]
),
\widetilde{H}
is positive semidefinite. It follows from
[
31
, Theorem 5.5.18]
that

\|\mathcal{P}^{(2)}\circ\widetilde{S}\circ\widetilde{H}\|\leq\|\mathcal{P}^{(2)}\circ\widetilde{S}\|\times\max_{1\leq i\leq 2r}\widetilde{h}_{ii}=\|\mathcal{P}^{(2)}\circ\widetilde{S}\|=\|\mathcal{P}^{(1)}\circ S\|.

Here,
S
is also a Cauchy matrix and hence, by
[
32
, Eq. (17)]
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}^{(1)}\circ S\|\leq\frac{\|\mathcal{P}^{(1)}\|}{\min_{i}|\lambda_{i}|^{1/2}+\min_{j}|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|^{1/2}}\leq|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}\|\mathcal{P}^{(1)}\|\leq|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}\|U^{\top}E\widehat{U}\|,

\displaystyle\|(JU^{\top}E\widehat{U}J)\circ S\|\leq\frac{\|U^{\top}E\widehat{U}\|}{\min_{i}|\lambda_{i}|^{1/2}+\min_{j}|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|^{1/2}}\leq|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}\|U^{\top}E\widehat{U}\|.

In summary, we have
\|T\|\leq 2|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}\|U^{\top}E\widehat{U}\|
. From
Lemma

5
,

\|U^{\top}EU\|\leq 4\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}+\frac{8}{3}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)

with probability at least
1-2n^{-\nu}
, where
\vartheta(\nu,r,n)=\nu\log n+r\log 9
.
Meanwhile, by the Davis–Kahan theorem,

\|U^{\top}E(I-UU^{\top})\widehat{U}\|\leq\|E\|\times\|(I-UU^{\top}\widehat{U}\|\leq\frac{2\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}}.

Combining the above bounds yields

\begin{split}\|T\|\leq|\lambda_{r}|^{-1/2}\Bigl(8\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}+\frac{16}{3}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)+\frac{4\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}}\Bigr)\end{split}

(B.10)

with probability at least
1-2n^{-\nu}
. Finally, if
\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{2}\geq\dots\geq\lambda_{r}\geq 0
then
r_{-}=0,U=U_{+},\mathcal{P}^{(1)}=0
, and
Eq.

B.10
can be improved to

\|T\|\leq\lambda_{r}^{-1/2}\Bigl(4\sqrt{\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}+\frac{8}{3}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)+\frac{2\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{r}}\Bigr)

(B.11)

with probability at least
1-2n^{-\nu}
. This completes the proof of
Lemma

6
.
∎

Lemma 7
.

Assume the setting in
Theorem

1
or
Theorem

2
. Then for any
k\in[n]
,

\begin{split}\|(I-UU^{\top})P^{k}EU\|_{2\to\infty}&=\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{k}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU\|_{2\to\infty}\\
&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{k}\|_{2\to\infty}\Bigl(\sqrt{8\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}+\frac{8}{3}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)\Bigr)\end{split}

with probability at least
1-n^{-(\nu-1)}
, where
\vartheta(\nu,r,n)=\nu\log n+r\log 5
.

Proof of
Lemma

7
.

Let
e_{i}
denote the
i
-th elementary basis vector in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and let
\xi_{i}
denote the
i
-th row of
U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{k}U_{\perp}^{\top}
. We have

\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{k}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU\|_{2\to\infty}=\max_{i}\|e_{i}^{\top}U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{k}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU\|=\max_{i}\|\xi_{i}^{\top}EU\|.

We shall bound
\|\xi_{i}^{\top}EU\|
using a standard
\epsilon
-net argument. To that end, for any
i
,

\|\xi_{i}^{\top}EU\|=\sup_{v\in\mathcal{S}}\xi_{i}^{\top}EUv,

where
\mathcal{S}=\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{r}\colon\|v\|=1\}
. Let
\mathcal{S}_{1/2}
be a
1/2
-cover of
\mathcal{S}
with respect to the
\ell_{2}
norm. Then, for any
v\in\mathcal{S}
and
w\in\mathcal{S}_{1/2}
with
\|v-w\|\leq 1/2
, we have

|\xi_{i}^{\top}EU(v-w)|\leq\|\xi_{i}^{\top}EU\|\cdot\|v-w\|\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\xi_{i}^{\top}EU\|,

and hence

\|\xi_{i}^{\top}EU\|=\sup_{v\in\mathcal{S}}\xi_{i}^{\top}EUv\leq\sup_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{1/2}}\xi_{i}^{\top}EUw+\frac{1}{2}\|\xi_{i}^{\top}EU\|.

Rearranging the above inequality gives

\|\xi_{i}^{\top}EU\|\leq 2\sup_{v\in\mathcal{S}_{1/2}}\xi_{i}^{\top}EUv.

Here, for any
w\in\mathcal{S}_{1/2}
, Bernstein’s inequality guarantees

\mathbb{P}(\xi_{i}^{\top}EUw\geq t)\leq\exp\Bigl(\frac{-t^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}+2Mt/3}\Bigr),

(B.12)

for
M=\|Uw\|_{\infty}\cdot\|\xi_{i}\|_{\infty}
and
\sigma^{2}\leq 2\rho_{n}\|\xi_{i}\|^{2}\cdot\|Uw\|^{2}=2\rho_{n}\|\xi_{i}\|^{2}
.

Observe that
\|Uw\|_{\infty}\leq\|U\|_{2\to\infty}
since
\|w\|=1
. Let
\vartheta(\nu,r,n)=\nu\log n+r\log 5
and

t_{*}=\frac{4M}{3}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)+\sqrt{2}\rho_{n}^{1/2}\|\xi_{i}\|\sqrt{\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}.

Consequently,

\frac{t_{*}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}+2Mt_{*}/3}\geq\vartheta(\nu,r,n),

and hence

P(\xi_{i}^{\top}EUw\geq t_{*})\leq\exp(-\vartheta(\nu,r,n))=n^{-\nu}\times 5^{-r}.

In the present setting,
|\mathcal{S}_{1/2}|\leq 5^{r}
holds by a volumetric argument (e.g., see
[
58
, Corollary 4.2.13]
). By taking a union bound over all
w\in\mathcal{S}_{1/2}
, we therefore obtain

P(\sup_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{1/2}}\xi_{i}^{\top}EUw\geq t_{*})\leq n^{-\nu}.

In summary, we have
\|\xi_{i}^{\top}EU\|\leq 2t_{*}
with probability at least
1-n^{-\nu}
. By taking another union bound over all
i\in[n]
, we obtain

\begin{split}\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{k}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU\|_{2\to\infty}&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{k}U_{\perp}^{\top}\|_{2\to\infty}\Bigl(\frac{8}{3}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)+\sqrt{8\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}\Bigr)\\
&\leq\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{k}\|_{2\to\infty}\Bigl(\sqrt{8\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}+\frac{8}{3}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)\Bigr)\end{split}

with probability at least
1-n^{-(\nu-1)}
, where the second inequality follows from
Eq.

1.1
. This completes the proof of
Lemma

7
.
∎

Lastly, we present a technical lemma for bounding
\|EU\|_{2\to\infty}
. The result here is a slight improvement compared to a direct application of matrix Bernstein’s inequality. See also

[
39
, Lemma 3.3]
.

Lemma 8
.

Consider the setting in
Theorem

1
through
Theorem

3
. Let
V
be a
n\times r
matrix independent of
E
. Denote by
e_{i}
the
i
-th elementary basis vector in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then, for any
i\in[n]
,

\|e_{i}^{\top}EV\|\leq 2\sqrt{2\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}\|V\|+\tfrac{8}{3}\|V\|_{2\to\infty}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)

(B.13)

with probability at least
1-n^{-\nu}
, where
\vartheta(\nu,r,n)=\nu\log n+r\log 5
.
Eq.

B.13
then implies

\|EV\|_{2\to\infty}\leq 2\sqrt{2\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}\|V\|+\tfrac{8}{3}\|V\|_{2\to\infty}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)

(B.14)

with probability at least
1-n^{-(\nu-1)}
.

Proof of
Lemma

8
.

We follow the same
\epsilon
-net argument as in the proof of
Lemma

7
. More specifically, let
\mathcal{S}=\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{r}\colon\|v\|=1\}
and
\mathcal{S}_{1/2}
be a
minimal

1/2
cover of
\mathcal{S}
. For any
i\in[n]
, we have

\|e_{i}^{\top}EV\|\leq 2\max_{i}\sup_{w\in\mathcal{S}_{1/2}}e_{i}^{\top}EUw.

Next, by Bernstein’s inequality, for any fixed but arbitrary
w\in\mathcal{S}_{1/2}
, we have

\mathbb{P}(e_{i}^{\top}EUw\geq t)\leq\exp\Bigl(\frac{-t^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}+2Mt/3}\Bigr),

where
M=\|Vw\|_{\infty}\leq\|V\|_{2\to\infty}
and
\sigma^{2}\leq 2\rho_{n}\|Vw\|^{2}\leq 2\rho_{n}\|V\|^{2}
. Now, define

t_{*}=\tfrac{4}{3}\|V\|_{2\to\infty}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)+\sqrt{2\rho_{n}\vartheta(\nu,r,n)}\|V\|.

Hence,

\mathbb{P}(e_{i}^{\top}EVw\geq t_{*})\leq n^{-\nu}5^{-r}.

Now, choose some
i\in[n]
. As
|\mathcal{S}_{1/2}|\leq 5^{r}
, taking a union bound over
\mathcal{S}_{1/2}
we obtain

\mathbb{P}(\|e_{i}^{\top}EV\|\geq 2t_{*})\leq n^{-\nu}

as claimed in
Eq.

B.13
.
Finally, taking a union over all
i\in[n]
, we obtain

P(\|EV\|_{2\to\infty}\geq 2t_{*})=P(\max_{i\in[n]}\|e_{i}^{\top}EV\|)\leq n^{-(\nu-1)},

as claimed in
Eq.

B.14
. This completes the proof of
Lemma

8
.
∎

Lemma 9
.

Consider the setting in
Theorem

1
through
Theorem

3
. Define

\sigma_{*}^{2}=\max\Bigl\{n\rho_{n}\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}^{2},n\rho_{n}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\lambda_{r+1}^{2}\Bigr\}

We then have

\|\Lambda_{\perp}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU\|\leq 2\sigma_{*}\sqrt{2\log n}+\frac{4}{3}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\cdot\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}\cdot\log n

(B.15)

with probability at least
1-n^{-(\nu-1)}
.

Similarly, if
P
is positive semidefinite then

\|\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU\|\leq 2\widetilde{\sigma}_{*}\sqrt{2\log n}+\frac{4}{3}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}\cdot\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}\cdot\log n

(B.16)

with probability at least
1-n^{-(\nu-1)}
, where now

\widetilde{\sigma}_{*}^{2}=\max\Bigl\{n\rho_{n}\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}^{1/2}\|_{2\to\infty}^{2},n\rho_{n}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\lambda_{r+1}\Bigr\}

Proof.

Let
v_{i}
denote the
i
-th column of
\Lambda_{\perp}U_{\perp}^{\top}
and
u_{i}
denote the
i
-th column of
U^{\top}
. Then

\Lambda_{\perp}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}e_{ij}v_{i}u_{j}^{\top}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}e_{ii}v_{i}u_{i}^{\top}+\sum_{i\not=j}e_{ij}(v_{i}u_{j}^{\top}+v_{j}u_{i}^{\top})

which is a sum of independent mean
0
random matrices.

Let
X_{ii}=e_{ij}u_{i}v_{i}^{\top}
and
X_{ij}=e_{ij}(v_{i}u_{j}^{\top}+v_{j}u_{i}^{\top})
.
Then

\|X_{ij}\|\leq L:=2\max_{ij}\|u_{i}\|\cdot\|v_{j}\|\leq 2\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}\times\|U\|_{2\to\infty}

almost surely.
Furthermore, for any matrices
M
and
N
we have
(M+N)^{\top}(M+N)\preceq 2M^{\top}M+2N^{\top}N
where
\preceq
denote the Lowner ordering for symmetric matrices. Hence

\begin{split}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[X_{ii}^{\top}X_{ii}]+\sum_{j\not=i}\mathbb{E}[X_{ij}^{\top}X_{ij}]&\preceq\sum_{i}\sum_{j}2\mathrm{Var}[e_{ij}](u_{j}v_{i}^{\top}v_{i}u_{j}^{\top}+u_{i}v_{j}^{\top}v_{j}u_{i}^{\top})\\
&\preceq 2\rho_{n}\sum_{i}\sum_{j}u_{i}u_{i}^{\top}\|v_{j}\|^{2}+u_{j}u_{j}^{\top}\|v_{i}\|^{2}\\
&\preceq 4n\rho_{n}\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\sum_{i}u_{i}u_{i}^{\top}\preceq 4n\rho_{n}\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\times I\end{split}

Similarly, we also have

\begin{split}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[X_{ii}X_{ii}^{\top}]+\sum_{j\not=i}\mathbb{E}[X_{ij}X_{ij}^{\top}]&\preceq\sum_{i}\sum_{j}2\mathrm{Var}[e_{ij}](v_{i}u_{j}^{\top}u_{j}v_{i}^{\top}+v_{j}u_{i}^{\top}u_{i}v_{j}^{\top})\\
&\preceq 2\rho_{n}\sum_{i}\sum_{j}v_{i}v_{i}^{\top}\|u_{j}\|^{2}+v_{j}v_{j}^{\top}\|u_{i}\|^{2}\\
&\preceq 4n\rho_{n}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\sum_{i}v_{j}v_{j}^{\top}\preceq 4n\rho_{n}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\times\Lambda_{\perp}^{2}\end{split}

Define

\sigma_{*}^{2}=\max\{n\rho_{n}\|U_{\perp}\Lambda_{\perp}\|_{2\to\infty}^{2},n\rho_{n}\|U\|_{2\to\infty}^{2}\lambda_{r+1}^{2}\}.

Then by a matrix Bernstein’s inequality
[
54
, Theorem 1.6]
we have

\mathbb{P}(\|\Lambda_{\perp}U_{\perp}^{\top}EU\|\geq t)\leq n\exp\Bigl(-\frac{t^{2}}{8\sigma_{*}^{2}+2Lt/3}\Bigr).

Taking
t=2\sqrt{2}\sigma_{*}\sqrt{\log n}+\frac{2}{3}L\log n
, we obtain the bound in
Eq.

B.15
. Derivation of
Eq.

B.16
follows the same argument and is thus omitted.
∎
